Big-Group Games — all full cards

Numbered and sorted by type. Includes the Daly Exercise games that were not already in the playbook (Bottle Flip, Hoop Hut, and the rest). Same-game titles live on one card. Written for a 30-minute class. No elimination. Soft tags. Sport games use the real ball.

Some games adapted from physedgames.com

All games · New Games · Warm Up Games · Dodgeball · Indigenous games · Large-group games

Large-group games · 67 games, all on the cards below
  • All 67 large-group games are on the cards below. Open “More details” on a card for the original overview, steps, source, and video.
  • Large group in this catalogue often means doubled classes (about 40–60) if you have the space. Shrink boundaries and tagger counts for a single class.
  • Safety defaults for throwing and kicking games: foam or soft balls, tags below the shoulders, no head shots, two-finger or noodle tags.
  • Dodgeball is optional programming. Prefer Detective, Net, Bench, or Prison Ball when you want more students moving; full house rules are on the Dodgeball page.

1. Alien Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, February · Slot: 16-25

Tagged aliens drop the waist hoop and do 5 jumping jacks to rejoin.

Equipment: Noodles; hoop per runner

Set-up: Hoops at waists. Chasers with noodles.

How we play

  1. Tag, drop hoop, 5 jacks, pick hoop up, rejoin.
  2. Freeze on the whistle.
Grades 1–2. Marching instead of jacks.
Grades 3–4. More chasers.
Grades 5–6. No hoops - bibs instead.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. Soft tags - never the head.

2. Angry Neighbour

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 1-5 · 5–8 min rounds.

Neighbours with pool noodles chase players off the yard, and tagged players go to a house mat, then re-enter. Use it to practise changing speed and safe noodle tagging.

Also called: On/Off

Equipment: pool noodles, exercise mats

Set-up: Lay exercise mats as houses along one side. The open floor is the yard. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Give 3–6 “neighbours” a pool noodle (the broom).
  2. Players start in the yard. Neighbours chase and tag with the noodle (legs/hips).
  3. Tagged players go to a house mat. Re-enter on a teacher call (“new yard!”) or a teammate high-five — pick one rule.
  4. Rotate neighbours every 60–90 seconds.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.
  • Noodle is a tap on a hip or shoe — never a swing at a head.

Variations

  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.
  • Teacher holds the only extra noodle in grades 1–2.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Angry Neighbour.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Noodles: demo a hip tap on a volunteer, then a wrong swing, then the hip tap again.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Angry Neighbour (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 1-5

Equipment: pool noodles, exercise mats

The neighbours (taggers) are not happy with the players in their yard, so they chase them with a broomstick (a pool noodle). Use it for quick, active tag play. Idea credit: James Barton.

  1. Lay exercise mats as houses along one side. The open floor is the yard.
  2. Give 3–6 “neighbours” a pool noodle (the broom).
  3. Players start in the yard. Neighbours chase and tag with the noodle (legs/hips).
  4. Tagged players go to a house mat. Re-enter on a teacher call (“new yard!”) or a teammate high-five — pick one rule.
  5. Rotate neighbours every 60–90 seconds.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Angry Neighbour for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Changes speed and pathway to avoid a tag in Angry Neighbour.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Angry Neighbour.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Angry Neighbour; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Angry Neighbour.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule. Teacher may hold the only extra noodle.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. Noodles tap hips or shoes; they are not swords. Walk on and off mats. No diving onto a house mat. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

Source

3. Backyard Dog Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · K-4 · 5–8 min rounds.

Each dog guards a small yard while visitors try to sneak in, and steal the bone if you use one, without being tagged. Use it to practise changing speed and safe tagging (idea from Deric Hafer).

Equipment: Cones, 4 foam balls

Set-up: Cone off several small “yards.” Place a foam ball (bone/toy) in each yard if you want a steal object. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Assign one dog per yard. Dogs may act like dogs but stay in or right beside their own yard.
  2. The rest of the class are visitors trying to sneak into a yard (and optionally steal the bone) without being tagged.
  3. A tagged visitor leaves that yard. Optional swap: the tagged player becomes the new dog.
  4. Play 45–90 second rounds and rotate dogs often.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Backyard Dog Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Backyard Dog Tag (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-4

Equipment: Cones, 4 foam balls

Some students are dogs (they may act like dogs if they want). The dogs try to chase people away from their yard. Idea credit: Deric Hafer.

  1. Cone off several small “yards.” Place a foam ball (bone/toy) in each yard if you want a steal object.
  2. Assign one dog per yard. Dogs may act like dogs but stay in or right beside their own yard.
  3. The rest of the class are visitors trying to sneak into a yard (and optionally steal the bone) without being tagged.
  4. A tagged visitor leaves that yard. Optional swap: the tagged player becomes the new dog.
  5. Play 45–90 second rounds and rotate dogs often.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Backyard Dog Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Changes speed and pathway to avoid a tag in Backyard Dog Tag.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Backyard Dog Tag.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Backyard Dog Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Backyard Dog Tag.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

4. Blob Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · 2–4 starters · optional noodles or cone hats

Tagged players join the blob. Reset before the last runner is hunted for minutes.

Also called: Home Free Bad Guy Blob Tag · Alien Tag

Equipment: Optional noodles or cone hats · spare kit at the side

Set-up: Mark the court. Starters bibbed or holding noodles. Spare kit at the side if the blob needs gear.

How we play

  1. Starters tag. Tagged player joins the blob (hold wrists, put on a cone hat, pick up a hoop).
  2. Only the agreed tag counts (noodle end, two fingers).
  3. When few runners remain, freeze, new starters.

If this happens

  • Chain breaks and still tags: no tag until it is linked.
  • Cone thrown as a hat: sit one turn.
  • Blob walls someone into a corner: open a corridor.

How a round ends. Last few runners, then reset. No one is out for the period.

Cues

  • Hold hands, do not yank.
  • Split at four.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Aliens: waist hoop, 5 jacks to rejoin if you prefer a non-blob re-entry.
  • Home Free Bad Guy Blob: a home hoop that resets a runner once.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Home Free Bad Guy Blob Tag; Alien Tag.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Blob Tag (PhysEdGames) · Home Free Bad Guy Blob Tag (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details: Home Free Bad Guy Blob Tag · Grades 1-6

Equipment: Hoops, pool noodles

A blob tag game with several roles and a lot happening at once. Idea credit: Dan Penna.

  1. Scatter hoops as short-stay “home free” bases (about 3 seconds max — no camping).
  2. 2–4 bad guys with pool noodles tag runners.
  3. A tagged runner joins the blob by holding hands/wrists with a bad guy. Only noodle ends tag; the chain may not break.
  4. Optional rescue: a free runner at a hoop calls HOME FREE and releases one blob player.
  5. Reset when the blob is huge or after 2–3 minutes.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Blob Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. The chain splits before it gets unsafe.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Blob Tag.
  • Skill focus. Holds on without yanking.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag or a catch — no argument that stalls Blob Tag.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

Source

5. Bottle Flip Challenge

Tag & chase

When: September, December · Slot: 16-25 · Daly Exercise · slot 16-25.

First upright cone-flip claims the centre ball and becomes the tagger. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Daly Exercise: Bottle Flip Challenge

Equipment: One cone per pupil; centre hoop; one soft ball

Set-up: Stand around a hoop. Soft ball inside. Each pupil holds a cone.

How we play

  1. On go, everyone flips their cone.
  2. First upright cone takes the ball and becomes it.
  3. Held-ball tag only. Tagged player does 5 jacks and stays in.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset kit, then the next round.

If this happens

  • Kit knocks a person: pause, widen space, restart.
  • Argument over a point: teacher call, play on.
  • Someone sits out after a miss: they rejoin on the next turn with a short task instead.

How a round ends. Play 3–6 minutes, or first team/player to the posted target, then rotate jobs.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • One job at a time. Extra players fetch or count.
  • A miss is a short task, then back in.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Flip onto a mat. Walking tag.
  • Jog tag. Two winners become two taggers.
  • Full reset each round.

Teaching tips

  • Source: Daly Exercise. Same house rules as the rest of the playbook.
  • Watch for crowding at one piece of kit. Add a second station before you add speed.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Bottle Flip Challenge for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the taught action in Bottle Flip Challenge with control, not a rush that knocks kit or people.
  • Skill focus. Chooses space, timing, or a partner job in Bottle Flip Challenge instead of crowding.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares kit in Bottle Flip Challenge.
  • Character Development. Honest about a miss or a tag in Bottle Flip Challenge.
  • Safety. Freeze on the whistle. Soft tags. No throws at a face.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a classmate re-enter after a miss or a tag.
Grades 1–2. Flip onto a mat. Walking tag.
Grades 3–4. Jog tag. Two winners become two taggers.
Grades 5–6. Full reset each round.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. A miss is a short task, then stay in.

6. Chicken Checkers

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Hoops or cones as bases · fewer bases than people

On ‘switch,’ leave your base and claim a new one. No base = you are it, or you do the short task.

Also called: Buzz Off / hoop free zones · Buzz Off · Hoop Dog

Equipment: Hoops or cones as bases

Set-up: Bases spread. One player per base to start if you can. Show the switch call.

How we play

  1. Call ‘switch.’ Everyone must leave and claim a new base.
  2. Two on one base: both leave.
  3. Player left without a base becomes the tagger, or does 5 jacks and stays in.

If this happens

  • Dive onto a cone: no claim.
  • Camping through the call: they must leave.

How a round ends. Rotate the leftover player. Play 4–6 switches.

Cues

  • One chicken per hoop.
  • No sliding a chicken into a person.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Stone Guard: raider steals a stone.
  • Tigers keep feet in hoops and tag from there (Hoop Dog).
  • Rest hoop you may step into (Buzz Off).

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Buzz Off / hoop free zones; Buzz Off; Hoop Dog.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Chicken Checkers for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. No sliding a piece into a person.
  • Skill focus. One object per trip.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Chicken Checkers; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag or a catch — no argument that stalls Chicken Checkers.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Must use a first name and a kind word to swap.

Safety. One extra person per hoop during the swap. No shoving out. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

7. Dead Ant Tag

Tag & chase

When: September, December · Slot: 0–5 or 16–25

Tagged players lie on their back like a dead ant. Four teammates each hold an arm or a leg to bring them back.

Equipment: None · optional pool noodles for taggers

Set-up: Open gym with a clear boundary. Choose 3–6 taggers; everyone else runs.

How we play

  1. A tagged player lies on their back with arms and legs in the air like a dead ant.
  2. To free them, four untagged players each hold one arm or one leg. The ant stands and all five are free.
  3. A rescuer who is tagged on the way becomes a dead ant too.
  4. Rotate taggers often. Freeze on the whistle, reset, next round.

Video. Dead Ant Tag (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-5

Equipment: None

A simple tag game. A tagged player lies on their back with arms and legs up, like a dead ant. To free them, four untagged players each hold an arm or a leg. Taggers cannot guard or tag the helpers. Works well with large groups. Have everyone spread out and choose a couple of taggers.

  1. Choose 3–6 taggers. Everyone else runs.
  2. A tagged player lies on their back with arms and legs in the air like a dead ant.
  3. To free them, FOUR untagged players must each hold one arm or one leg. Then the ant stands and all five are free.
  4. If a rescuer gets tagged on the way, they become a dead ant too.
  5. Rotate taggers often.

Source · Video

Grades 1–2. Walk-tag first. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic or a second rescue rule.

Safety. Soft two-finger tags on the back or shoulder, never the head. Clear boundary and freeze signal first. Rotate taggers so the same students are not always chasing. Rescuers hold wrists or ankles gently — no pulling.

8. Duck Duck Dash

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 4–6 taggers · 45–90 second rounds · 8–12 minutes total.

Run this standing-circle chase like Duck, Duck, Goose, where both players stay in after the chase. Use it to practise a quick start and a safe chase.

Equipment: None.

Set-up: Standing circle — sitting makes this too slow for a 30-minute class. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Walker taps shoulders: “duck… duck… dash!”
  2. The named player chases once around. The walker tries to take the open spot.
  3. Whoever is left walking starts the next tap. Nobody sits out.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. A tagged player takes the next role or uses the game’s re-enter rule right away — nobody sits out.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Tap the shoulder.
  • One lap.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Walk for 1–2.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Duck Duck Dash.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • ‘Duck… dash!’ Chase once. Both stay in.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Duck Duck Dash for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Chases one lap and returns.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Duck Duck Dash.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Duck Duck Dash; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Duck Duck Dash.
  • Safety. Shoulder tap, not the head.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Tap the shoulder, not the head. Circle stays still. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

9. Everybody’s It

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · optional one soft ball or spot markers

Anyone may tag. A tag has a small cost. Then you keep playing.

Also called: Clothespin Tag · Hot Potato · Pizza Tag

Equipment: Optional one soft ball or spot markers · optional clothespins

Set-up: Court marked. Soft two-finger tags. Show the cost.

How we play

  1. On go, tag anyone.
  2. If two people tag at once, RPS. The slower pose does 3 squats. Both stay in.
  3. Keep playing until the whistle.

If this happens

  • Hard tag: warning, then sit 10 seconds.
  • Ball version thrown at a head: switch to hold-and-touch only.

How a round ends. Timed rounds. No winner required.

Cues

  • Everyone tags.
  • RPS, then both stay in.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Hot Potato: only the person holding the ball may tag, by touch, no throw.
  • Pizza: spot marker on one hand; knock other pizzas off.
  • Clothespin: steal one pin from a back, clip it on yourself.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Clothespin Tag; Hot Potato; Pizza Tag.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Everybody’s It (PhysEdGames) · Everyone’s IT Frozen Tag (The Physical Educator)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 1-8

Equipment: None

A classic tag game. Everyone is it, and everyone can be tagged. A tagged player sits where they were tagged and stays down until the player who tagged them is tagged; then they get back up. Expect lots of running. If players sit too long, call “free pass” so they can get back up. If two players tag each other at the same time, they play Rock, Paper, Scissors to see who sits.

  1. Everybody is it AND everybody can be tagged. No equipment.
  2. If you are tagged, sit where you were tagged. You stay down until the person who tagged you gets tagged — then you stand up.
  3. Lots of running. If people sit too long, the teacher calls “free pass” and everyone stands.
  4. If two players tag each other at the same time, they play Rock-Paper-Scissors to see who sits.

  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).

10. Freedom Catch

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 1-6 · 5–8 min rounds.

Taggers freeze runners, and a teammate frees a frozen player by completing a throw and catch to them. Use it with large groups to practise tagging, throwing and catching together (game by Jennifer Holub).

Also called: Catch and Thaw Tag

Equipment: Cones, foam balls

Set-up: Large coned field. Several taggers; free players have foam balls. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Taggers freeze runners in place with hands ready to catch.
  2. A teammate frees a frozen player by completing a catch to them.
  3. A drop leaves the player frozen (or freezes the thrower — pick one and teach it).
  4. Timed rounds; rotate taggers. Built for 50+ if space is big.
  5. Large coned field.
  6. Several taggers; free players have foam balls.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Catch and Thaw Tag: one thrower carries the only soft thaw ball and cannot be tagged; a catch frees the frozen player, who becomes the new thrower (taggers use a foam stick).

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Freedom Catch.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

  2. Taggers freeze runners in place with hands ready to catch.
  3. A teammate frees a frozen player by completing a catch to them.
  4. A drop leaves the player frozen (or freezes the thrower — pick one and teach it).
  5. Timed rounds; rotate taggers. Built for 50+ if space is big.

12. Frozen Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 0–5 · 2–4 taggers · optional noodles · an agreed freeze shape and rescue

Tag freezes you in a shape. A teammate frees you. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Hospital Tag · Hot Dog Tag · Banana Tag · Bug Tag / Dead Ant · Bug Tag · Dead Ant · Waspital · Package Tag · Jack Frost · Sun and Ice · Caterpillar Tag · Frog Tag

Equipment: Optional noodles · optional hoops for Hospital

Set-up: Show the freeze shape once. Show the rescue once. Soft tag only.

How we play

  1. Taggers chase. Tagged player freezes in the shape and stays there.
  2. A free teammate does the rescue (high-five, hoop-over, peel, bun, copy-the-dance, four-person carry).
  3. Both are free. Out of bounds = frozen.

If this happens

  • Statue moves: they stay frozen.
  • Rough rescue: switch the whole class to a two-finger touch.
  • All frozen: new taggers, restart.

How a round ends. Timed rounds. Rotate taggers every 60–90 seconds.

Cues

  • Freeze tall — eyes up.
  • Unfreeze is a two-hand tap, not a shove.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Default: arms out, high-five.
  • Hospital: cover the spot, third tag = walk a hoop lap and rejoin.
  • Hot Dog: stand still, two classmates are the bun.
  • Banana: curve the body, two-hand peel.
  • Package (December): sit with hands in a bow, classmate ‘opens’ you.
  • Jack Frost: hug or high-five melt.
  • Bug / Dead Ant: freeze on your back with legs up; a teammate taps both feet.
  • Waspital: same hospital rule with a wasp story.
  • Unmask Tag (Daly Silly Bananas): one foot up, hands on head, teammate touches the hands. Do not call this Silly Bananas.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Hospital Tag; Hot Dog Tag; Banana Tag; Bug Tag / Dead Ant; Bug Tag; Dead Ant; Waspital; Package Tag; Jack Frost; Sun and Ice; Caterpillar Tag; Frog Tag.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Banana Tag (PhysEdGames) · Everyone’s IT Frozen Tag (The Physical Educator)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details: Banana Tag · Grades K-5

Equipment: None

A simple tag game. Tagged players curve their bodies into a banana shape. Another player “peels” them to set them free.

  1. Choose taggers. Everyone else is a runner.
  2. A tagged player must curve their body into a banana shape and hold it.
  3. A free teammate “peels” them (a two-hand sweep down the sides, agreed and appropriate) to free them.
  4. Rotate taggers. Keep tags gentle.

Source · Video

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Frozen Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Soft tag; the tagged player has a way back in.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Frozen Tag.
  • Skill focus. Someone helps unfreeze or walks a classmate to ‘hospital.’
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag or a catch — no argument that stalls Frozen Tag.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Must be unfrozen by two high-fives.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

13. Icebergs

Tag & chase

When: February · Slot: 16–25

Hoop islands that shrink. Same family as Sharks and Dolphins.

Equipment: 8–12 hoops.

Set-up: Scatter hoops.

How we play

  1. Travel between icebergs on the call.
  2. Remove one hoop each round.
  3. Share an iceberg. Nobody sits out.
Grades 1–2. Walk. Two per hoop.
Grades 3–4. Jog.
Grades 5–6. One hoop, two feet only.

Safety. Feet in hoops, no diving.

14. Kangaroos and Crocodiles

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 0–5 · Two teams · a centre line · two home lines

Hear your name. Turn. Get home. The other team tags.

Also called: Kangaroo Tag

Equipment: None · two home lines and a centre line

Set-up: Teams on a centre line, facing you. Homes behind each.

How we play

  1. Call ‘Kangaroos’ or ‘Crocodiles.’
  2. The named team turns and runs (or hops) home. The other team tags.
  3. Tagged players do 5 jumps and re-enter.

If this happens

  • False start: send that team back.
  • Wrong team runs: they are taggable.

How a round ends. Play several calls. No one sits out.

Cues

  • Wait for the name.
  • Tagged players jump 5 and re-enter next call.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Hop only (Kangaroo Tag).
  • What’s-the-time: jump the called number toward the caller, flee when they turn.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Kangaroo Tag.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

  • Movement Skill Development. Tagged player re-enters or switches — nobody sits out.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Kangaroos and Crocodiles.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Kangaroos and Crocodiles; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Kangaroos and Crocodiles.
  • Safety. Moves only when their name or shape wins.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Add bib tails instead of a body tag.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. 15. Line Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 run-skill game · Hall lines · 3–4 taggers · optional noodles

Travel only on painted lines. Leave a line and you are tagged.

Also called: Line Tag / Pac-Man · Pac-Man · Block Tag · Line / Block Tag

Equipment: Painted gym lines · optional noodles

Set-up: Everyone on a line before go. Show legal direction changes.

How we play

  1. Move forward or back on the lines. You may not step through a person on the same line.
  2. Tag = sit on the line as a block.
  3. A teammate high-fives to free you.
  4. Off a line = tagged.

If this happens

  • Shortcut across open floor: tagged.
  • Block stands up early: sit back down.
  • Jam at a crossing: freeze, sort, restart 10 seconds.

How a round ends. Timed rounds. Rotate taggers.

Cues

  • Stay on the paint.
  • No jumping to a new line.
  • Eyes up at corners.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Noodle taggers.
  • Crawl-through-legs free instead of a high-five.
  • Pac-Man: only travel on lines, same freeze-and-free.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Line Tag / Pac-Man; Pac-Man; Block Tag; Line / Block Tag.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

16. Massive 10 Second Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 1-6 · 5–8 min rounds.

Everyone is a tagger during short bursts of about 10 seconds, and tagged players take a knee until you bring them back in. Use it with large groups to practise quick changes of speed and gentle tags (adapted from Joel Sweetland’s Ten-Second Tag).

Equipment: none

Set-up: Everyone spreads out. On GO, EVERYONE is it for about 10 seconds. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Gentle two-finger shoulder tags only. You can tag others even if you get tagged during the burst.
  2. Whistle ends the burst. Anyone tagged in that burst sits or takes a knee.
  3. Start the next burst with whoever is still standing. Continue until a few remain, then reset sitters back in.
  4. Bursts can be 7–12 seconds — keep the flow, not the stopwatch. Split the gym if numbers are huge.
  5. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Massive 10 Second Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Massive 10 Second Tag (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 1-6

Equipment: none

A large-group version of Joel Sweetland’s 10-second tag. The cycles do not have to be exactly 10 seconds; add or take away a few seconds to keep the game flowing.

  1. Everyone spreads out. On GO, EVERYONE is it for about 10 seconds.
  2. Gentle two-finger shoulder tags only. You can tag others even if you get tagged during the burst.
  3. Whistle ends the burst. Anyone tagged in that burst sits or takes a knee.
  4. Start the next burst with whoever is still standing. Continue until a few remain, then reset sitters back in.
  5. Bursts can be 7–12 seconds — keep the flow, not the stopwatch. Split the gym if numbers are huge.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Massive 10 Second Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Changes speed and pathway to avoid a tag in Massive 10 Second Tag.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Massive 10 Second Tag.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Massive 10 Second Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Massive 10 Second Tag.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

17. Mystery Number Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 2-6 · 5–8 min rounds.

Hand out folded notes so a few students secretly become taggers when you say GO, then reshuffle the notes every 1–2 minutes. Use it to practise changing speed and safe tagging (idea from Chantal Dubois).

Equipment: sticky notes, or pieces of paper

Set-up: Prepare sticky notes. A few say IT (or a special number); the rest are blank/free. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Every player gets a folded note. They look only when you say GO.
  2. ITs tag. Runners flee. Tagged runners freeze until a free player high-fives them, or they become IT (pick one).
  3. After 1–2 minutes, reshuffle notes so new mystery taggers appear.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. Prepare sticky notes.
  6. A few say IT (or a special number); the rest are blank/free.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.
  • Blade or stick stays below the knee. No slapshots.

Variations

  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.
  • Feet-only hockey-ball version if sticks are new or the class is tired.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Mystery Number Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Sticks: teach grip and ‘blade down’ in the hallway line before the first dribble.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Mystery Number Tag (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 2-6

Equipment: sticky notes, or pieces of paper

Players do not know who the taggers are until the game starts. Expect lots of running. Idea credit: Chantal Dubois.

  1. Prepare sticky notes. A few say IT (or a special number); the rest are blank/free.
  2. Every player gets a folded note. They look only when you say GO.
  3. ITs tag. Runners flee. Tagged runners freeze until a free player high-fives them, or they become IT (pick one).
  4. After 1–2 minutes, reshuffle notes so new mystery taggers appear.

  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Mystery Number Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Mystery Number Tag.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch hospital, or role swap).

18. Octopus

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 6–8 min · 1 octopus to start.

Runners cross the gym past the octopus, and tagged players plant their feet and become seaweed taggers. Use it to practise dodging and changing speed.

Equipment: Two end lines.

Set-up: One octopus in the middle. Runners on one end. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. On go, runners cross.
  2. Tagged players stand with feet planted and become seaweed. They may tag with their arms.
  3. When most are seaweed, pick new octopi. Seaweed stretch and re-enter as runners.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Seaweed plants both feet.
  • Arms tag — the body stays on the spot.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Hoop octopus: the tagger holds a hoop as ‘tentacles’ — tag only with the hoop.
  • Dribble octopus: soccer ball at the feet while crossing.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Octopus.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Runners on one end line. One octopus in the middle.
  • On “Octopus!” everyone crosses. Tagged players become seaweed: feet planted where they were tagged, arms may tag the next wave.

Video. Ollie Ollie Octopus (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Octopus for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Crosses on the call, not after a wait.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Octopus.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Octopus; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Octopus.
  • Safety. Tagged player re-enters next wave — no last-one-standing.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Must cross with a skip or slide.

Safety. Feet stay planted for seaweed. No trips. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

19. Package Tag

Tag & chase

When: December · Slot: 16-25

Tagged player sits as a package (hands in a bow). A classmate opens them to rejoin.

Equipment: Pool noodles

Set-up: Taggers with noodles.

How we play

  1. Tag = sit criss-cross, hands in a bow.
  2. A classmate opens the package to free them.
  3. Freeze on the whistle.
Grades 1–2. Standing package.
Grades 3–4. More taggers.
Grades 5–6. No noodles.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. Soft tags - never the head.

20. Pony Roundup

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · K-6 · 5–8 min rounds.

Herders tag galloping ponies with a noodle, and free ponies join hands to rescue a captured pony from a stable. Use it as a galloping tag-and-rescue game (idea from Angela Crepeele).

Equipment: Hoops, pool noodles

Set-up: Scatter hoops as stables. Give 3–6 herders pool noodles. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Everyone else is a pony and gallops around the gym.
  2. Herders tag ponies with a noodle touch on the hip or shoulder (not the head).
  3. A tagged pony must stand inside the nearest stable (hoop).
  4. Free ponies may rescue by joining hands and galloping a captured pony out of the stable without being tagged.
  5. Play 1–2 minute rounds and switch herders. Upbeat music optional.
  6. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  7. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.
  • Noodle is a tap on a hip or shoe — never a swing at a head.
  • One extra person in a hoop during a swap — no shoving out.

Variations

  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.
  • Teacher holds the only extra noodle in grades 1–2.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Pony Roundup.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Noodles: demo a hip tap on a volunteer, then a wrong swing, then the hip tap again.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. PE Games – Pony Roundup (video by PhysEdGames) (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-6

Equipment: Hoops, pool noodles

A galloping tag-and-rescue game. Idea credit: Angela Crepeele.

  1. Scatter hoops as stables. Give 3–6 herders pool noodles.
  2. Everyone else is a pony and gallops around the gym.
  3. Herders tag ponies with a noodle touch on the hip or shoulder (not the head).
  4. A tagged pony must stand inside the nearest stable (hoop).
  5. Free ponies may rescue by joining hands and galloping a captured pony out of the stable without being tagged.
  6. Play 1–2 minute rounds and switch herders. Upbeat music optional.

  • Movement Skill Development. Changes speed and pathway to avoid a tag in Pony Roundup.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Pony Roundup.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Pony Roundup; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Pony Roundup.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule. Teacher may hold the only extra noodle.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. Noodles tap hips or shoes; they are not swords. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

21. Shadow Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Pairs or whole class · 4 min · sun or poly-spot ‘shadows’.

Players tag by stepping on a shadow or a floor spot, never a body. Use it to practise changing speed and pathway.

Equipment: Poly spots if you have no sun.

Set-up: Outdoor shadows, or indoor spots as pretend shadows. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Show the boundary, the freeze signal, and who starts as tagger before GO.
  2. Tag by stepping on a shadow or spot.
  3. Tagged player becomes the tagger, or does 5 jumps and re-enters.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. Play Shadow Tag in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Soft feet on the shadow.
  • Switch taggers often.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Pairs: stay in your partner’s shadow.
  • Spot-shadows only.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Shadow Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Outdoors: tag by stepping on a shadow. Indoors: each player drags a poly-spot on a string or just plays freeze-tag on spots.
  • Soft feet. No stomps on ankles.

  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Shadow Tag.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Shadow Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Shadow Tag.
  • Safety. No stomps on ankles.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Must tag and say the person’s name.

22. Sharks and Dolphins

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · 2 sharks · noodles · marked sea

Sharks tag. Dolphins freeze, then get rescued.

Also called: Sharks and Dolphins (chute) · Shark and Fish

Equipment: 2 noodles · marked sea · optional parachute

Set-up: Sea boundary. Two sharks. Show the freeze (T or agreed shape) and the rescue.

How we play

  1. Sharks tag with noodles.
  2. Tagged dolphin freezes.
  3. A free dolphin rescues them (under the arms, high-five, or chute peek).
  4. Out of bounds = frozen.

If this happens

  • Freeze shape not clear: no rescue until it is.
  • Shark guards one frozen player all game: send them hunting.
  • Rough rescue: high-five only.

How a round ends. Sharks win if all are frozen at the whistle. Otherwise most free dolphins.

Cues

  • Cross on the call — do not wait on the line.
  • Safe once both feet are over the end line or in a hoop.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Parachute cat-and-mouse.
  • More sharks.
  • Shark and Fish: same freeze-and-rescue.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Sharks and Dolphins (chute); Shark and Fish.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Sharks and Minnows (Mrs. Carly Glanzman)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

  • Active Living. Stays in Sharks and Dolphins for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Crosses on the call, not after a wait.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Sharks and Dolphins.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Sharks and Dolphins; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag or a catch — no argument that stalls Sharks and Dolphins.
  • Safety. Tagged player re-enters next wave — no last-one-standing.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Add a rescue: a free dolphin high-fives a newly tagged shark to bring them back once.

Safety. Feet only in hoops — no diving. One or two per hoop for older grades. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

23. Slap Rover

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 5 min · two end lines.

A visitor walks over, slaps one hand on the other team’s line, and races home before the slapped player can tag them. Use it to practise a quick reaction and a safe chase.

Equipment: Cones for two team lines.

Set-up: Two teams on opposite lines. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. One team calls a name: that player walks over with style.
  2. The receiving team holds out hands. The visitor slaps one hand, then sprints home.
  3. The slapped player chases. If the visitor makes it, the chaser joins them. If tagged, the visitor switches teams.
  4. Take turns down the line so everyone is called.
  5. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  6. A tagged player takes the next role or uses the game’s re-enter rule right away — nobody sits out.
  7. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Cross on ‘rover come over.’
  • Soft tag. Re-enter next call.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Send a colour only.
  • Must skip across.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Slap Rover.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • A classic crossing game. Taggers in the middle. Call ‘Red rover, red rover, send everyone over’ (or a colour, or a clothing detail).
  • Tagged players become taggers, then stretch and re-enter as runners after two crossings so it does not become last-one-standing.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Slap Rover for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Crosses on the call, not after a wait.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Slap Rover.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Slap Rover; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Slap Rover.
  • Safety. Tagged player re-enters next wave — no last-one-standing.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk both ways. Teacher calls names in order.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Beat-box from the calling team.

Safety. Slap hands only, not arms. No diving tags. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

24. Squirrel’s Tail

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 outdoor · Pairs or whole class · 5 min · pinnies as tails.

Four squirrel teams steal nuts from the middle or pull tails, and a player without a tail pays two nuts to buy it back. Use it outdoors with Grades 3–6.

Equipment: Fabric tails, hoops, beanbags (nuts), 4-colour pinnies.

Set-up: Four squirrel teams. Nuts in the middle. Each player has a tail. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Steal a nut home, or pull one tail.
  2. A player with no tail is frozen until they pay two nuts to buy it back.
  3. Count nuts at the whistle.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. A tagged player takes the next role or uses the game’s re-enter rule right away — nobody sits out.

Cues

  • Two fingers on the tail.
  • Hand it back.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Mother squirrel may only walk.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Squirrel’s Tail.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Same family as Tail Tag. One squirrel with a tail, or everyone with a tail.
  • Steal and return, or clip the tail on yourself and become the squirrel.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Squirrel’s Tail for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Hands the pin or tail back.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Squirrel’s Tail.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Squirrel’s Tail; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Squirrel’s Tail.
  • Safety. Two fingers on the tail or pin, not on clothes.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Outdoor, walk-jog. Teacher-formed teams.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Tails tuck loosely. No grabbing clothing. Grade 3+ only. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

25. Survivor Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 0–5 · Whole class · 5 min · 2–3 taggers.

Tagged players do a named skill, such as 5 jumps or a stretch, then re-enter. Use it to practise safe tagging with no elimination.

Equipment: 2–3 pinnies.

Set-up: Choose taggers. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Show the boundary, the freeze signal, and who starts as tagger before GO.
  2. Tagged players do a named skill (5 jumps or a stretch) then re-enter.
  3. Change taggers each minute.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. Play Survivor Tag in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • A tag is a skill, then you are back in.
  • Change taggers each minute.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Skill is a balance until a helper taps.
  • Two skills on the second tag.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Survivor Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Tagged players do a named skill (5 jumps, a stretch, a log roll on a mat) then re-enter.
  • Do not play last-survivor. The game is the movement.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Survivor Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. A tag is a short skill, then back in.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Survivor Tag.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Survivor Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Survivor Tag.
  • Safety. Soft tag.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Skill is a skip-30 or a peer stretch.

Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.
