27. Ten-Second Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 4–6 min · new taggers every 10 s.

Send a new small group of taggers in every 10 seconds, and tagged runners do 5 jumping jacks, then keep running. Use it for short run-and-recover bursts so no one is the tagger for long.

Equipment: Centre line of cones, whistle.

Set-up: Split the gym. Taggers on one side, runners on the other. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. On the first whistle, a small group of taggers enters the runner half.
  2. Every 10 seconds, whistle again. Those taggers freeze. A new group goes in.
  3. A tagged runner does 5 jumping jacks on the spot, then keeps running.
  4. Rotate so every student is a tagger once.
  5. Play Ten-Second Tag in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Soft tag.
  • Jacks, then back in.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Walk-tag for 1–2.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Ten-Second Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Half gym. Whistle starts a tagger group. Next whistle: they freeze, a new group goes.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Ten-Second Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Runs or walks on the whistle.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Ten-Second Tag.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Ten-Second Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Ten-Second Tag.
  • Safety. Soft tag; tagged player re-enters after 5 jacks.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Soft tag. Taggers wait behind the line until the whistle. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

28. Tidy Up

Tag & chase

When: September, June · Slot: 8-15

Chase a moving bucket and return balls one at a time.

Equipment: Scattered tennis balls; one bucket

Set-up: Scatter balls. One holder walks or jogs with the bucket overhead.

How we play

  1. On go, collect one ball, chase the holder, place it in the bucket, repeat.
  2. Freeze on the whistle.
Grades 1–2. Two buckets.
Grades 3–4. Walking holder for infants.
Grades 5–6. Colour balls for two teams.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. Soft tags - never the head.

29. Tripod Tag

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 5 min.

In groups of four, three players hold hands and turn to protect a named teammate from the outside tagger. Use it to practise teamwork and quick footwork.

Equipment: None.

Set-up: Groups of 4 in their own space. Three join hands. Name one player in the circle as the target. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. The outsider tries to tag the named player.
  2. The circle may only rotate — they screen with their bodies, no pushing out.
  3. Short rounds. Switch the tagger and the target each time.
  4. A tagged player takes the next role or uses the game’s re-enter rule right away — nobody sits out.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Safe shape is three points on the floor.
  • 3-second hold, then you must move.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Safe shape is a stork.
  • Must change the three points each time.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Tripod Tag.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • A runner is safe in a tripod (two feet and one hand, or two hands and one foot) for 3 seconds, once at a time.
  • Taggers cannot wait beside a tripod. Tagged players do 5 jumps and re-enter.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Tripod Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. A tag is a short skill, then back in.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Tripod Tag.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Tripod Tag; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Tripod Tag.
  • Safety. Soft tag.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. Joined hands stay at waist height. No dragging the target. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

30. Tunnel Freeze

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 4–6 taggers · 45–90 second rounds · 8–12 minutes total.

Tagged players freeze in a wide stance until a classmate frees them. Use it to practise safe tagging and a fair way back into the game.

Equipment: None.

Set-up: 3–5 taggers in pinnies. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Tagged player freezes with a wide stance.
  2. Grades 1–2: a friend high-fives to unfreeze.
  3. Grades 5–6 only, if both students agree: a friend may walk through the stance. Anyone may say no and use a high-five instead.
  4. Change taggers every 90 seconds.
  5. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.

Cues

  • Freeze wide.
  • High-five unless both agree to a walk-through.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Hospital tag if the class is crowded.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Tunnel Freeze.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • 1–2: high-five only. 5–6: optional tunnel with consent.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Tunnel Freeze for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Soft tag; a way back in.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Tunnel Freeze.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Tunnel Freeze; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Tunnel Freeze.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Accepts a ‘no’ to the tunnel.
Grades 1–2. Walk-tag. Teacher plus one student tagger. 30–45 second bursts. Re-enter with a high-five or the game’s hospital rule.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Optional tunnel; consent first.

Safety. No crawling under a moving person. Head up. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

31. Wake-Up Bear

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · 1 bear · 45 s rounds.

The class chants until the bear wakes on a secret count, then the bear chases runners toward the walls. Use it to practise listening, a quick start and a short chase.

Equipment: A circle of cones. No blindfold.

Set-up: Bear sits in the middle facing away. Runners on the circle. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Teacher whispers a number 1–12 to the bear, or holds fingers the bear can see.
  2. The circle chants “Wake up, bear!” The bear stands on the secret count.
  3. Bear tags toward the walls. Tagged players do 5 jacks and become a second bear for the next round.
  4. Change the bear every 45 seconds.
  5. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  6. A tagged player takes the next role or uses the game’s re-enter rule right away — nobody sits out.
  7. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Bear faces away.
  • Chase to the wall.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • No blindfold — ever.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.
  • Tagged players become helpers (peelers, medics, or extra taggers) instead of sitting.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Wake-Up Bear.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Chant. Bear stands on the secret count. Tagged: 5 jacks, then a turn as bear.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Wake-Up Bear for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Runs to a wall, not a pile.
  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Wake-Up Bear.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares space in Wake-Up Bear; does not crowd a hospital or jail.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Wake-Up Bear.
  • Safety. Bear is not blindfolded.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).
Grades 1–2. Walk chase. Teacher is the count.
Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Full speed with two-finger tags. Add one tactic (fake, safe-zone worth one use, or blob split). Students can referee a boundary.

Safety. No blindfold. Chase to the wall, not into a pile. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

32. Waspital

Tag & chase

When: January · Slot: 0–5

Everyone-is-it tag with a stretch hospital.

Equipment: 1–2 hoops.

Set-up: Hoops are the waspital.

How we play

  1. Everyone is it. Soft two-finger tag.
  2. Two tags = walk to the hoop, stretch, re-enter.
Grades 1–2. Walk-jog.
Grades 3–4. Jog.
Grades 5–6. Students lead the hospital stretch.

Safety. Cap at 5 minutes. No head tags.

33. Wolf’s Den

Tag & chase

When: December, September · Slot: 16–25 · 2 wolves · 3 crossings, then reset.

Runners cross a middle zone past the wolves, and tagged players help tag for one crossing, then rejoin. Use it to practise changing speed with no one out for the period.

Equipment: Two end lines and a 3-m middle strip.

Set-up: Two wolves in the den. Everyone else on one end. Use the basketball lines as the boundary; cones mark any safe zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. On “Cross!” runners try to reach the far line without a tag.
  2. Tagged: 5 jumping jacks at the side, then help tag for one crossing only.
  3. Reset wolves every three crossings so the den does not fill forever.
  4. Rotate taggers every 45–90 seconds so the same three students are not chasing all period.
  5. A tagged player takes the next role or uses the game’s re-enter rule right away — nobody sits out.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Tag only in the den.
  • Jacks, then one turn as a helper.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head.
  • Change pathway. Do not run someone over to make a tag.
  • If you are tagged, show it honestly and take the next job.

Variations

  • Walk for 1–2.
  • Large-group setup from PHYSEDGAMES: This is the standard (width-of-the-gym) version for about 15–30 players. For 40–60 use Large Group Wolf’s Den instead.
  • Large-group (PHYSEDGAMES): run the den the LENGTH of the gym so 40–60 can play. More wolves. Rabbits run end to end. Pinnies on new wolves.
  • Walk-tag first, then jog. Add a second tagger only when space is clean.
  • Safe islands: three hoops that freeze a tagger for three seconds if a runner reaches one.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Wolf’s Den.
  • Watch for head-down sprints and pile-ups at a safe zone. Pause, spread out, restart.
  • Year plan: December, September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Cross on the call. Tagged help for one crossing. Reset wolves.
  • PHYSEDGAMES large-group setup lives on this card — no separate game.

Video. Large Group Wolf’s Den (PhysEdGames) · Wolf’s Den (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details: Large Group Wolf’s Den · Grades 1-6

Equipment: scarves (or pinnies)

A large-group version of Wolf’s Den. Depending on your space, it can work with 40 to 60 players, so it suits two or more classes together. It can also run outdoors, with cones marking the den. For a regular group of 15–30 players, the den usually runs across the width of the gym; for a large group, run it along the length.

  1. Mark a long, narrow den down the LENGTH of the gym (the large-group setup). Wolves must stay inside that strip.
  2. Pinny several wolves (more wolves for 40–60 players).
  3. Rabbits start on one end line. On GO they run through the den to the far end without being tagged.
  4. Two-finger tags. Tagged rabbits become wolves (put on a scarf) or visit a crate/jail until the next wave.
  5. After each wave, rabbits turn and run back through. Add an optional crate in the den for dropped scarves.
  6. Play 3–5 minutes, then choose new starting wolves. Outdoors: cone the den.

Source · Video

More details · Grades K-6

Equipment: None

The wolf moves around the den and tries to tag the rabbits as they run back and forth through it. Use lines or cones to mark the den in the middle, between two safe areas. The wolf must stay in the den. Rabbits run through the den from side to side without being tagged. A tagged rabbit becomes a wolf; keep pinnies handy so new wolves can put one on quickly. Play until all of the rabbits are caught. Works in the gym, outside or at camp.

  1. This is the standard (width-of-the-gym) version for about 15–30 players. For 40–60 use Large Group Wolf’s Den instead.
  2. Mark a den strip across the WIDTH of the gym. 2–4 wolves stay in the den.
  3. Rabbits start on one end, run through the den to the other end, then back on the next signal.
  4. Tagged rabbits become wolves or sit a turn. Wolves may not leave the den.
  5. Play several waves, then new wolves.

Source · Video

  • Skill focus. Uses space, a fake, or a safe zone instead of a pile-up in Wolf’s Den.
  • Skill focus. Re-enters after the stretch.
  • Character Development. Honest about a tag, a catch, or a line — no argument that stalls Wolf’s Den.
  • Safety. Soft two-finger or noodle tag on the back or shoulder — never the head. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a tagged classmate re-enter (high-five, stretch, hospital, or role swap).

Grades 3–4. Jog-tag. 3–5 taggers. 60–90 second rounds. Soft two-finger or noodle tags. Rotate every round.
Grades 5–6. Wolves may only tag in the den.

34. 3-Ball Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · K-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Play soccer with three balls at once so more students get touches. Use it with large classes to practise moving into space and keeping your head up.

Equipment: 3 soccer balls

Set-up: Play soccer with THREE balls at once. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Use regular soccer rules otherwise (feet, out of bounds, goalies optional).
  2. Any ball that enters the goal counts. Restart that ball at centre or from the goalie.
  3. Heads up — three balls means more traffic. Foam balls in a gym. Ideal for large classes.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in 3-Ball Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. 3-Ball Soccer (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-8

Equipment: 3 soccer balls

A large soccer game with three balls in play instead of one, so players get more touches. It suits large classes.

  1. Play soccer with THREE balls at once.
  2. Use regular soccer rules otherwise (feet, out of bounds, goalies optional).
  3. Any ball that enters the goal counts. Restart that ball at centre or from the goalie.
  4. Heads up — three balls means more traffic. Foam balls in a gym. Ideal for large classes.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in 3-Ball Soccer for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the inside of the foot or a taught kick; looks up before sending.
  • Skill focus. Moves to space after a pass; does not crowd the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. No slide tackles; foam or school soccer ball as listed.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

Source

35. 4-Goal Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 2-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Four teams each defend one net and may score in any other net. Use it with large groups to practise attacking and defending in more than one direction.

Equipment: Soccer balls, 4 goals

Set-up: Set 4 nets, one on each side of a square or gym. Four teams, each defending one net. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Teams may score in ANY net except their own.
  2. Use 2–3 soccer balls for a large group. Agree on goalie rules and whether a team can score in the same net twice in a row.
  3. Play timed quarters and optionally rotate which net each team defends.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in 4-Goal Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. 4-Goal Soccer (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 2-8

Equipment: Soccer balls, 4 goals

A soccer game for larger groups. Set up 4 nets, one on each side, for 4 teams. Teams can score in any net except their own. Use whatever soccer rules you choose. It gives lots of movement and skill practice.

  1. Set 4 nets, one on each side of a square or gym. Four teams, each defending one net.
  2. Teams may score in ANY net except their own.
  3. Use 2–3 soccer balls for a large group. Agree on goalie rules and whether a team can score in the same net twice in a row.
  4. Play timed quarters and optionally rotate which net each team defends.

  • Skill focus. Moves to space after a pass; does not crowd the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. No slide tackles; foam or school soccer ball as listed.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.

36. 5 Soccer Circle Ideas

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 4-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Use these five soccer circle activities, from simple to more advanced, to practise passing and dribbling. Run them as rotating stations or one at a time with the whole class.

Equipment: Soccerballs, cones

Set-up: Make several circles of 6–10 players with cones and plenty of soccer balls. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Idea 1 — Circle pass: pass around the circle with 2-touch limits.
  2. Idea 2 — Inside-out: one or two players in the middle try to intercept; swap on a steal.
  3. Idea 3 — Through-gate: add a cone gate and require passes through it.
  4. Idea 4 — Dribble-in / pass-out: players take turns dribbling into the circle and passing out.
  5. Idea 5 — Two-ball circle: two balls at once for extra touches.
  6. Run as 5 rotating stations or do one idea at a time with the whole class.
  7. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  8. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in 5 Soccer Circle Ideas.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Equipment: Soccerballs, cones

  1. Make several circles of 6–10 players with cones and plenty of soccer balls.
  2. Idea 1 — Circle pass: pass around the circle with 2-touch limits.
  3. Idea 2 — Inside-out: one or two players in the middle try to intercept; swap on a steal.
  4. Idea 3 — Through-gate: add a cone gate and require passes through it.
  5. Idea 4 — Dribble-in / pass-out: players take turns dribbling into the circle and passing out.
  6. Idea 5 — Two-ball circle: two balls at once for extra touches.
  7. Run as 5 rotating stations or do one idea at a time with the whole class.

37. Bench Ball

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 3-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Teams throw a foam ball to teammates standing on their bench, and each catch lets another teammate climb on. Use it to practise throwing, catching and moving into space.

Equipment: 2 benches, dodgeballs

Set-up: Two teams. A bench at each far end. Start with 1–2 teammates already on your bench. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Stay on your half. Throw a foam ball so a teammate ON THE BENCH catches it.
  2. Every legal catch lets one new teammate climb onto the bench.
  3. Defence may intercept or block throws. First team to get all (or a target number) of its players on the bench wins.
  4. This is the catch-onto-the-bench game — not Bench Dodgeball.
  5. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Bench Ball.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Bench Ball (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 3-8

Equipment: 2 benches, dodgeballs

A low-organization throwing and catching game. Each team tries to be first to get all of its players onto its bench. A player gets onto the bench when a teammate already on the bench catches their throw, so every catch adds another player. Teams can guard and block to make it harder. Set up one bench on each side of the gym and make 2 teams. One player from each team starts on the bench at the far end. Add the balls. The first team to reach the target (for example, 6 players on the bench) wins. Play many rounds for lots of throwing and catching practice.

  1. Two teams. A bench at each far end. Start with 1–2 teammates already on your bench.
  2. Stay on your half. Throw a foam ball so a teammate ON THE BENCH catches it.
  3. Every legal catch lets one new teammate climb onto the bench.
  4. Defence may intercept or block throws. First team to get all (or a target number) of its players on the bench wins.
  5. This is the catch-onto-the-bench game — not Bench Dodgeball.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Bench Ball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Sends, receives, or travels with control using the taught skill (foot, hand, or implement).
  • Skill focus. Uses space: spread out, fake, or change speed to get past a defender.
  • Skill focus. Honest tags; no puppy-guarding a flag or hoop.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. No body checks; throws stay away from faces.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. Foam dodgeballs. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

38. Booger Ball

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams · 6 min · sticky-name send.

A tagged ball-carrier must pass and cannot shoot on that possession. Use it with Grades 5–6 as an invasion game that rewards passing.

Equipment: 1 basketball or playground ball, pinnies, a net and/or hoop.

Set-up: Two teams. Mark a crease in front of each goal. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Move the ball by passing. A tagged carrier must pass and cannot shoot on that possession.
  2. Score in the net (1) or a hoop (2).
  3. No contact except a soft tag on the carrier.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • The ball ‘sticks’ to the catcher — they must pass in 3 seconds.
  • Uses the real sport ball.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must pass with the other hand.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Booger Ball.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Whoever catches is ‘it’ with the ball and cannot run. They pass to unstick.
  • Score by a catch in a hoop or by tagging a cone.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Booger Ball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. 3-second hold, then pass.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out, passes then moves, or defends a line — one tactic, not a crowd around the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a name before the catch.
Grades 1–2. Skip this game. Use End Zone Ball instead.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

39. Captain Ball

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams · 6–8 min · one captain per team in a hoop.

Teams pass the ball to their captain, who stays in a hoop. Use it to practise passing and getting open.

Equipment: Soccer or playground ball, pinnies, 2 hoops.

Set-up: Each team has a captain in a hoop. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Pass toward your captain. Captain may catch but not run.
  2. Three completes, or a catch in the hoop, scores.
  3. Rotate the captain.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Captain stays in the hoop.
  • Three completes to score.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Two captains.
  • Captain must volley or bounce-pass out.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Captain Ball.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Captain may catch only — no running, no leaving the hoop.
  • Field players pass to the captain. Three successful captain-catches = a point, then rotate the captain.

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

  • Active Living. Stays in Captain Ball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Shows the named locomotor or shape.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out, passes then moves, or defends a line — one tactic, not a crowd around the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Changes movement on the first signal.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Walking. Teacher may be the first captain.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Must pass twice before the captain catch.

40. Capture the Egg

Invasion & team

When: May · Slot: 16–25

Carry a beanbag through traffic.

Equipment: Beanbags, pinnies.

Set-up: Two ends. A few taggers in the middle.

How we play

  1. Carry an egg across.
  2. If tagged, hand the egg to the tagger and switch jobs.
  3. Count fair crossings.
Grades 1–2. Walk. Wide channel.
Grades 3–4. Jog.
Grades 5–6. Two eggs at once.

Safety. Soft tag. No diving on the egg.

41. Capture The Flag Outdoors

Invasion & team

When: June, May · Slot: 16–25 outdoor

The standard Capture the Flag on a big outdoor field: hide your flag, steal theirs, bring it home.

Equipment: 2 flags, cones, a large outdoor area

Set-up: Split a large outdoor area in half. Two teams, one flag each. Teams get about a minute to hide the flag on their own half.

How we play

  1. No guarding right over your flag.
  2. On GO, try to steal the other flag and carry it back across the middle line.
  3. Tagged on the other half: go to jail. A teammate frees you with a tag (see the video for their jail sequence).
  4. Add house rules for boundaries and hiding limits. Watch the video before the first play.

Video. Capture The Flag Outdoors (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 3-8

Equipment: 2 Flags, large playing area

The standard Capture the Flag game for PE class. Find a large area (field, community centre, playground, bushes or forest) and make 2 teams. Teams take a minute to hide their flag on their half (no peeking), then play. Each team tries to capture the other team’s flag and bring it across the middle line. A player tagged on the other side goes to jail for 2 minutes; the tagger taps the jail (a bench or similar) and then goes back into play. No guarding right over the flag. Add your own rules as needed; the PHYSEDGAMES video shows more detail. Credit: set up by Paul Grosskopf.

  1. Find a large outdoor area and split it in half. Two teams, one flag each.
  2. Give teams about a minute to hide their flag on their own half (no peeking). No “puppy-guarding” the flag.
  3. On GO, teams try to steal the other flag and carry it back across the middle line.
  4. If you are tagged on the opponent’s half, go to jail for 2 minutes. A teammate can free you by tagging the jail object after a tagger taps it — follow the video for the exact jail sequence used at their school.
  5. Add house rules as needed (boundary, flag-hide limits). Watch the official video before the first play.

Source · Video

Grades 1–2. Walk-through first. Small field, one object, teacher referees.
Grades 3–4. Full rules, larger area. Rotate roles every round.
Grades 5–6. Add strategy talk between rounds. Students set house rules.

Safety. Soft two-finger tags on the back or shoulder, never the head. Clear boundary and freeze signal first. Rotate taggers so the same students are not always chasing. Outdoor boundaries you can see; no running near fences or roads.

42. Collect the Treasure

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · 4 teams · 5–6 min · beanbags in the middle.

Teams carry treasure home one piece at a time, and a tagged player walks the piece back to the middle. Use it to practise moving into space and simple attack and defence jobs.

Equipment: Beanbags, 4 hoops.

  3. No standing in your hoop to guard.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

  • One treasure at a time.
  • Tagged = walk it back.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must crab-walk.
  • Steal from other nests after the middle is empty.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Collect the Treasure.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Beanbags in a centre hoop. Teams carry one at a time to a home hoop.
  • Tagged players walk the treasure back to the middle. No guarding the hoop.

Alberta PEW outcomes

  • Active Living. Stays in Collect the Treasure for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Sends, receives, or travels with control using the taught skill (foot, hand, or implement).
  • Skill focus. One object or one rescue at a time.
  • Skill focus. Walks the treasure back after a tag, without arguing.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the treasure.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Two treasures allowed if you pair up.

43. Cornerball

Invasion & team

  3. Defend by intercepting.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

  • Score = catch in any corner hoop.

  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must visit two hoops.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.

Teaching tips

  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Score by a catch in any of four corner hoops.
  • The carrier cannot run. Defence intercepts without contact.

Alberta PEW outcomes

  • Active Living. Stays in Cornerball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Carrier pivots — no running with the ball.
  • Skill focus. Looks for a teammate before a score.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.

44. Crab Football

Invasion & team

When: September, October · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams · 3 × 60-second rounds.

Players crab-walk and push or carry the ball to the line in short rounds. Use it to build core strength and teamwork.

Equipment: Playground ball, two end lines.

Set-up: Two teams. Short court. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Travel in a crab walk. Push or carry the sport ball to the line.
  2. 30–45 second rounds, then stretch.
  3. Swap if wrists are tired.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Hips up. Short bouts for wrists.
  • Uses the real sport ball.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must pass to a crab teammate.
  • One-hand carry only.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Crab Football.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September, October — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Crab-walk and carry or kick a playground ball to a line.
  • 60 seconds, then stand, shake wrists, switch.

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

  • Active Living. Stays in Crab Football for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Hips up for a short bout.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out, passes then moves, or defends a line — one tactic, not a crowd around the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Crab a short distance, then walk the rest.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Must pass once while still in crab.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

45. End Ball

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams of 6–10 · 2 × 4 min · sport ball, two end zones.

The carrier cannot run and must pass, and a clean catch by your end-zone catcher scores. Use it as a passing game in the same family as Captain Ball.

Equipment: Football or playground ball, pinnies, cones for two end zones.

Set-up: Each team names one catcher who stays in the far end. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Carrier cannot run and must pass in 5 seconds.
  2. A clean catch in the end zone scores. Rotate the catcher.
  3. Defend by intercepting, not by grabbing the holder.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Pivot, don’t run.
  • 5-second hold. Pass to space.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must complete 3 passes before a score (The Perfect Pass).
  • Walking only for Grade 1–2.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in End Ball.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • End zones are 2–3 metres deep (mats or cones).
  • Score with a catch in the far end zone. The carrier cannot run — pivot only.

Video. Endball (Newt's PE Games)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in End Ball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Carrier pivots — no running with the ball.
  • Skill focus. Looks for a teammate before a score.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Walking. Teacher may be catcher.
Grades 3–4. Three-pass rule before a score.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

46. End Wall Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 3-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Each team’s goal is the whole end wall, so the game works with a large group. Use it to practise passing and shooting with an indoor soccer ball or Softex foam ball (idea from CIRA Ontario).

Equipment: indoor soccer ball or Softex foam ball, pinnies

Set-up: Make two teams (pinnies on one side). Play the full gym or a large field. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Each team’s GOAL is the entire end wall / end line — no small net required. That is what makes this work with a crowd.
  2. Play soccer rules with an indoor soccer ball or Softex foam ball: feet only. Decide in advance whether a goalie is allowed to use hands.
  3. A goal counts when the ball hits the opponent’s end wall or fully crosses the end line (choose one and stick to it).
  4. Restart at centre after each goal. Add a second foam ball if numbers are very high so more players get touches.
  5. Play 4–6 minute periods and rotate roles.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Blade or stick stays below the knee. No slapshots.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.
  • Feet-only hockey-ball version if sticks are new or the class is tired.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in End Wall Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Sticks: teach grip and ‘blade down’ in the hallway line before the first dribble.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. End Wall Soccer (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 3-8

Equipment: indoor soccer ball or Softex foam ball, pinnies

A soccer game that works well with large groups. Idea credit: CIRA Ontario (https://www.ciraontario.com/).

  1. Make two teams (pinnies on one side). Play the full gym or a large field.
  2. Each team’s GOAL is the entire end wall / end line — no small net required. That is what makes this work with a crowd.
  3. Play soccer rules with an indoor soccer ball or Softex foam ball: feet only. Decide in advance whether a goalie is allowed to use hands.
  4. A goal counts when the ball hits the opponent’s end wall or fully crosses the end line (choose one and stick to it).
  5. Restart at centre after each goal. Add a second foam ball if numbers are very high so more players get touches.
  6. Play 4–6 minute periods and rotate roles.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in End Wall Soccer for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the inside of the foot or a taught kick; looks up before sending.
  • Skill focus. Moves to space after a pass; does not crowd the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. No slide tackles; foam or school soccer ball as listed.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer. Feet-only if sticks are new.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. Sticks stay below the knee. No slapshots. Blade on the floor when you stop. 47. End Zone Ball

Invasion & team

When: September, October · Slot: 16–25 · Same family as End Ball · 2 × 4 min.

Players roll, not throw, the ball to teammates in the end zone while the other team tries to intercept. Use it to practise accurate sending and intercepting.

Equipment: 4–8 footballs or soccer balls, pinnies, optional mats as obstacles.

Set-up: Two halves. Each team names 2–3 end-zone catchers who stay in their end. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Players in their half roll balls to their own catchers.
  2. The other team tries to intercept rolls.
  3. A clean catch in the end zone scores.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Move after you pass.
  • Eyes up before you throw.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Two balls for a big class.
  • Score only in a hoop inside the end zone.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in End Zone Ball.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September, October — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Flag-football shape with a sport ball or beanbag.
  • 3-second or 5-second hold. No running with the ball.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in End Zone Ball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Carrier pivots — no running with the ball.
  • Skill focus. Looks for a teammate before a score.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Add a 3-second hold and a must-pass rule.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. Walk on and off mats. No diving onto a house mat. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

48. Four Corner Flags

Invasion & team

When: September, October · Slot: 16–25 · Four teams · 8–12 min · one hoop and 2–3 flags per corner.

Four teams steal flags one at a time from other hoops and are safe in their own quadrant. Use it to practise attacking and defending space.

Equipment: 16 beanbags (4 colours), 4 hoops, 4 pinnies colours, cones.

Set-up: Gym in four quadrants. Each team’s flags start in its hoop. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Steal one flag at a time from another hoop and return it home.
  2. You are safe in your own quadrant. Tagged in another quadrant: walk the flag back and return home.
  3. No standing over your own hoop (no puppy-guarding).
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Safe in your own quarter.
  • One flag at a time. Walk a jail rescue.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Two-team Capture the Flag: one flag each, prison, walk-home rescue.
  • Capture the cone: steal standing cones instead of pinnies.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Four Corner Flags.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September, October — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Split the gym into four. Each team has a hoop (flag nest) and a jail cone.
  • Start with 2 flags of each other colour in every nest, or 3 like-coloured flags to steal.

Video. 4-way Capture the Flag (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Four Corner Flags for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Sends, receives, or travels with control using the taught skill (foot, hand, or implement).
  • Skill focus. One object or one rescue at a time.
  • Skill focus. Walks the flag back after a tag, without arguing.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the flag.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Outdoor map-optional version in May.

Safety. No body checks. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

49. Fruit Salad

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 5 min · hoops or spots in a circle + one extra player.

On a called fruit, those players switch corners while the middle player tries to claim an empty one. Use it to practise listening and moving quickly into open space.

Equipment: Four corner spots.

Set-up: Name each corner a fruit. One player in the middle. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Call a fruit. Those players switch corners.
  2. The middle player tries to claim an empty corner.
  3. ‘Fruit salad’ = everyone switches.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Swap on your fruit.
  • Fruit salad is everyone.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Add a locomotor (skip to your new spot).
  • Use sport names.
  • Large-group setup from PHYSEDGAMES: Give taggers different-coloured pinnies. As a class, name a fruit for each colour and a freeze shape for that fruit (apple = round; banana = curve).
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Fruit Salad.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Give every seated or standing player a fruit name (4 names). One player in the middle.
  • Call a fruit — those players swap spots. Middle player steals a spot. The leftover is the new middle.

Video. Fruit Salad (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-5

Equipment: Pinnies (different colours)

A tag game for Kindergarten to Grade 5. Give the taggers pinnies in different colours, and have students name fruits that match those colours. A student tagged by a certain colour freezes in the shape of the matching fruit. Decide as a class how to freeze as each fruit and how to unfreeze it, for example apple – cut, banana – peel, blueberry – wash, watermelon – roll. You can also try a vegetable theme. Idea credit: Curtis Glasgow.

  1. Give taggers different-coloured pinnies. As a class, name a fruit for each colour and a freeze shape for that fruit (apple = round; banana = curve).
  2. Also agree how each fruit gets unfrozen (apple is “cut,” banana is “peeled,” blueberry is “washed,” watermelon is “rolled,” etc.).
  3. Taggers tag. A tagged player freezes in that fruit’s shape until the matching unfreeze action is done by a free teammate.
  4. Play short rounds and let the class invent a vegetable version next time.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Fruit Salad for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Finds a group or a spot without diving.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out and moves to an open corner — not a crowd at one spot.
  • Skill focus. Leftovers stay in — they join the teacher or the next round.
  • Character Development. Honest about who reached the spot first; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a classmate’s name; includes a player who has not had a turn in the middle.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. First foot on the spot keeps it. No shoving. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

50. Go for Goal

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Stations or 1v1 · 8 min.

Small teams of 2–3 pass once before shooting at cone goals, and waiting players act as walls or the next wave. Use it to practise passing and shooting.

Equipment: Soccer balls, cone goals, pinnies.

Set-up: 2–3 a side. Extra players are waiting walls or next wave. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Name the two jobs (attack / defend or the two end-lines) and which ball we are using.
  2. Pass once before a shot.
  3. Rotate every 2 minutes.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.
  5. Play Go for Goal in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Head up.
  • Shoot from a marked arc.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must shoot with the weak foot or off-hand.
  • One-touch finish.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Go for Goal.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Small cone goals. 1v1 or 2v2. Rotate after a goal or after 45 seconds.
  • Use hockey sticks only if you already taught a safe grip and the ball is a hockey ball or a puck.

51. Guard the Gates

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams or 4v4 waves · 4–6 minute periods · extra players are walls, bumpers, or the next wave.

Attackers dribble or pass through cone gates while defenders try to win the ball with their feet only. Use it to practise ball control and fair defending.

Equipment: Cone gates, soccer balls, pinnies.

  • Several cone gates. Attackers score by dribbling through a gate. Defenders intercept the ball, not the person.
  • After a score, the ball goes back to the attack start.
