53. Home Alone

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · K-4 · 5–8 min rounds.

Each homeowner guards a cone key in a hoop while thieves try to steal it without being tagged. Use it to practise awareness, attacking and defence.

Equipment: Hoops, cones

Set-up: Lay out about 8 hoops. One player stands in each hoop with a cone — that cone is the KEY to the house. They must protect it. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Everyone else is a thief and tries to steal a key without being tagged by the player in that hoop.
  2. If tagged, try a different house. If the steal succeeds, thief and homeowner swap roles.
  3. House players may tag only while keeping one foot in (or next to) their hoop.
  4. Play short rounds so many students get to be homeowners.
  5. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Home Alone.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Home Alone (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-4

Equipment: Hoops, cones

A game for awareness, strategy, attacking and defence. Lay out 8 hoops and put 1 player in each. Give each of them a cone (or other item): it is the key to their house, and they must protect it. Everyone else is a thief and tries to take a key without being tagged by the player in that hoop. A tagged thief tries another house. A thief who takes a key swaps places with the player in the hoop. Idea credit: Joe Defreitas.

  1. Lay out about 8 hoops. One player stands in each hoop with a cone — that cone is the KEY to the house. They must protect it.
  2. Everyone else is a thief and tries to steal a key without being tagged by the player in that hoop.
  3. If tagged, try a different house. If the steal succeeds, thief and homeowner swap roles.
  4. House players may tag only while keeping one foot in (or next to) their hoop.
  5. Play short rounds so many students get to be homeowners.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Home Alone for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Sends, receives, or travels with control using the taught skill (foot, hand, or implement).
  • Skill focus. Uses space: spread out, fake, or change speed to get past a defender.
  • Skill focus. Honest tags; no puppy-guarding a key or hoop.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Two-finger or noodle tag.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not had a turn as homeowner.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No body checks. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

Source

54. Hurley Burley

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Grades 3–6 · 8 min · one sport ball.

Players may kick, bounce, throw or run a few steps, but a soft tip means they must pass right away. Use it to practise quick decisions in an invasion game.

Equipment: Soccer or playground ball, two goals, pinnies.

Set-up: Two teams. Teacher tosses in. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. You may kick, bounce, throw, or run a few steps.
  2. If tipped (soft two-finger), you must pass immediately.
  3. Throw-in = 1, strike-in = 2, kick-in = 3.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • A tip means you must pass at once.
  • Kick-in goal 3, hit 2, throw 1.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Hurley Burley.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Any legal send: kick, bounce, or throw. If the ball is only tipped, that player must pass immediately.
  • Score into a cone goal. Keep a simple 3–2–1 tally.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Hurley Burley for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Chooses kick, bounce, or throw.
  • Skill focus. A tip means pass at once.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Hands and kick only. Walking.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Three-pass rule before a shot.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

55. Numbers Hockey

Invasion & team

When: November · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams numbered · 8 min · puck, sticks if taught.

Call two or three numbers, and those players play to one goal while the rest wait at the side. Use it for short, frequent hockey turns.

Equipment: Floor-hockey sticks or hands, hockey ball, two cone goals.

Set-up: Two teams sit at the side, numbered. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Name the two jobs (attack / defend or the two end-lines) and which ball we are using.
  2. Call two or three numbers. Those players play to one goal.
  3. Sit and call a new set.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Your number only.
  • Sticks down on the whistle.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Call two numbers always.
  • Must pass once.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Numbers Hockey.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Sticks: teach grip and ‘blade down’ in the hallway line before the first dribble.
  • Year plan: November — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Teams sit on opposite sidelines, numbered 1–n. Call one or two numbers. Those players play 1v1 or 2v2 to a small goal.

Video. The Numbers Game (hockey) (Prime Coaching Sport)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Numbers Hockey for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Ball stays close; trap or dribble under control.
  • Skill focus. One ball at a time; looks before sending.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer. Feet-only if sticks are new.
Grades 3–4. Floor-hockey sticks below the waist.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. Sticks stay below the knee. No slapshots. Blade on the floor when you stop. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

56. Pin Galore Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25

Soccer where every pin is a goal. Knock one down and carry it back to your own row.

Equipment: Plastic pins, indoor soccer balls or Softex foam balls

Set-up: Each team lines plastic pins along its end line. Two or more indoor soccer balls or Softex foam balls.

How we play

  1. Play soccer with feet only.
  2. Score by knocking over an opponent’s pin instead of shooting into a net.
  3. Carry a knocked-down pin back and add it to your own row.
  4. The game ends when one team has every pin, or at time (most pins wins).

Video. Pin Galore Soccer (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 1-8

Equipment: Plastic pins, indoor soccer balls or Softex foam balls

A soccer variation that increases participation. Instead of scoring in a goal, players try to knock over the other team’s pins. A player who knocks down a pin brings it to their own side and adds it to their team’s pins. The game ends when one team has all the pins or time runs out. Use it as a soccer lead-up game.

  1. Each team lines plastic pins on its end. Use indoor soccer balls or Softex foam balls.
  2. Play soccer (feet only). Instead of a net, you score by knocking over an opponent’s pin.
  3. When you knock a pin down, carry that pin back and add it to your own row.
  4. Game ends when one team has every pin, or when time expires (most pins wins).
  5. Use it as a soccer lead-up; every pin is a scoring chance.

Source · Video

Grades 1–2. Walk-through first. Small field, one object, teacher referees.
Grades 3–4. Full rules, larger area. Rotate roles every round.
Grades 5–6. Add strategy talk between rounds. Students set house rules.

Safety. Foam balls. Kicks stay low — no blasting from close range. Nobody stands in front of their own pins as a goalie wall.

Invasion & team

Knock the noodle off the house cone; defenders intercept.

58. Reverse Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams · 6–8 min · soccer ball, two goals.

Field players may only tap or pass with their hands, and keepers save with their feet. Use it as an invasion game that flips the usual soccer rules.

Equipment: Soccer ball, pinnies, two cone goals.

Set-up: Two teams. One keeper each. Goals can face out if you want a harder angle. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Field players may only tap or pass with hands — no carrying.
  2. Keepers save with feet only.
  3. Score in the other team’s goal. Rotate keepers every 2 minutes.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.
  5. Play Reverse Soccer in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Hands tap or pass — no carry.
  • Keepers use feet only.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Everyone touches before a shot.
  • Walking only.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Reverse Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Field players may only send with hands (volleyball tap or two-hand pass). They cannot run with the ball.
  • Keepers save with feet. Rotate keepers every 2 minutes.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Reverse Soccer for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Ball stays close; trap or dribble under control.
  • Skill focus. One ball at a time; looks before sending.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Skip or play walking hand-ball.
Grades 3–4. Must pass once before a shot.
Grades 5–6. Everyone on the team touches before a shot.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

59. Rob the Nest

Invasion & team

When: September, January · Slot: 16–25 · Centre pile of objects · team hoops as nests · one object per trip

Get objects into your nest. One per trip.

Also called: Rob the Nest (dribble) · Capture the Egg · Capture the Flag · Bucket Ball · Robin’s Nest · Capture the Ball · Fishing · Capture the Cone

Equipment: Centre pile of objects · team hoops as nests

Set-up: Objects in the middle or in other nests. Teams at their hoop. Show the carry or dribble you want.

How we play

  1. First player runs in, takes one object, gets it into the nest, tags.
  2. Wrong action (two objects, no dribble if required) = object goes back.
  3. Later round: you may steal from another nest. No standing in a hoop to guard it.

If this happens

  • Two hands on the same object: first hand keeps it.
  • Centre traffic jam: one way in, other way out.
  • Puppy-guarding the nest: send them out.

How a round ends. Most objects in the nest, or first to a target number.

Cues

  • One ball at a time.
  • No standing in a hoop.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Dribble a ball (Rob the Nest).
  • Hook cones with a noodle (Fishing).
  • Outdoor flag and a jail.
  • Carry an egg; tagged = hand it over.
  • Bucket Ball: drop in a bucket instead of a hoop.
  • Robin’s Nest: same one-per-trip rule.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Rob the Nest (dribble); Capture the Egg; Capture The Flag Outdoors; Capture the Flag; Bucket Ball; Robin’s Nest; Capture the Ball; Fishing; Capture the Cone.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Rob the Nest (Prime Coaching Sport)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

  • Movement Skill Development. Ball stays close; trap or dribble under control.
  • Skill focus. One ball at a time; looks before sending.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.

61. Sideline Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 2-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Each team lines its sideline as a wall of goalies while five players from each side play on the floor, then rotate every 2 minutes. Use it with large groups so everyone is either playing or goalkeeping.

Equipment: Foam Balls

Set-up: Each team lines its entire sideline as a wall of goalies. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. The first 5 players from each line step onto the floor and play soccer with 2–3 foam balls at once.
  2. If a ball is kicked through a sideline, that is a save / miss — put it straight back in. Do not stop play for celebrations.
  3. Every 2 minutes, the 5 floor players go to the BACK of their goalie line and the next 5 step on.
  4. Works well for large groups because everyone is either playing or goalkeeping.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Sideline Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Sideline Soccer (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 2-8

Equipment: Foam Balls

Each team lines up along its sideline as a row of goalies. The first 5 players from each line enter and play soccer with 2–3 balls at the same time. When a goal is scored, put the ball straight back in play; do not stop. After 2 minutes, blow the whistle: the 5 players on the floor go to the back of their team’s line, and the next 5 come in. Use it to work on soccer skills with large groups.

  1. Each team lines its entire sideline as a wall of goalies.
  2. The first 5 players from each line step onto the floor and play soccer with 2–3 foam balls at once.
  3. If a ball is kicked through a sideline, that is a save / miss — put it straight back in. Do not stop play for celebrations.
  4. Every 2 minutes, the 5 floor players go to the BACK of their goalie line and the next 5 step on.
  5. Works well for large groups because everyone is either playing or goalkeeping.

  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the inside of the foot or a taught kick; looks up before sending.
  • Skill focus. Moves to space after a pass; does not crowd the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. No slide tackles; foam or school soccer ball as listed.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.

62. Slappers!

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · 4-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Four teams strike bouncy balls with an open hand into any other team’s corner net. Use it to practise striking, moving into space and defending a net.

Equipment: 4 nets, 4 bouncy balls (ex racquetball ball)

Set-up: 4 teams, 4 nets in the 4 corners, 1 net per team. Toss in several bouncy balls (racquetballs or similar). Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Players SLAP / strike balls with an open hand to send them into any opponent net.
  2. No picking the ball up, no running with it, no throwing or catching — striking only.
  3. Players may move, defend their net, and set up teammates.
  4. A slap that goes in an opponent net scores. Play timed quarters and rotate net corners.
  5. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Slappers!.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Slappers! (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 4-8

Equipment: 4 nets, 4 bouncy balls (ex racquetball ball)

A striking game (swatting the ball with an open hand). Four teams, four nets in the corners, one per team. Toss in a few balls; players slap them to score in other teams’ nets. Players may move, defend, attack and set up teammates, but they cannot pick up the ball, run with it, or throw or catch it.

  1. 4 teams, 4 nets in the 4 corners, 1 net per team. Toss in several bouncy balls (racquetballs or similar).
  2. Players SLAP / strike balls with an open hand to send them into any opponent net.
  3. No picking the ball up, no running with it, no throwing or catching — striking only.
  4. Players may move, defend their net, and set up teammates.
  5. A slap that goes in an opponent net scores. Play timed quarters and rotate net corners.

  • Skill focus. Moves to space after a pass; does not crowd the ball.

63. Star Wars

Invasion & team

When: November, January · Slot: 16-25

The beach ball is the only target. People are not targets.

Equipment: Beach ball, many soft balls, a bench at each end

Set-up: Beach ball on the centre line. Teams behind their lines.

How we play

  1. Throw soft balls at the beach ball.
  2. Stay behind your line.
  3. Beach ball touches the other bench = 1.
Grades 1–2. Must roll, not throw.
Grades 3–4. Throw at the beach ball only.
Grades 5–6. Three teams or a must-roll rule.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle.

64. Steal the Bacon

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Two numbered lines · one object in the middle

Your number is called. Beat the other player to the object and get home.

Also called: Oyster Shell · Sneaky Races · Reaction Speed

Equipment: One object in the middle · two numbered lines

Set-up: Two teams on opposite lines, same numbers. Object centre.

How we play

  1. Call a number (or two). Those players race the object.
  2. First hand on it tries to get back over their line. The other may tag.
  3. Soft tag only. Then reset.

If this happens

  • False start: send them back.
  • Two numbers collide: dead ball, replay.
  • Object kicked away: replace and redo.

How a round ends. First team to 5 clean returns, or play a set list of numbers.

Cues

  • Grab the object, not the person.
  • Soft tag the carrier.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Oyster: one beanbag on the centre line.
  • Sneak & React: tiptoe on ‘sneak,’ sprint home on the clap.
  • Drop-catch at chin height instead of a sprint.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Oyster Shell; Sneak & React; Sneaky Races; Reaction Speed.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Oyster Shell (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details: Oyster Shell · Grades 2-8

Equipment: 1 beanbag, floor lines (or cones)

A reaction and running game for all ages that needs little equipment. Split the group into 2 teams (they do not need to be even). With cones, you can play it outside too.

  1. Two teams on opposite sidelines facing a centre line. Teams do not need even numbers.
  2. Place one beanbag on the centre line (the oyster).
  3. Number the players on each side. Call a number; those two sprint to the beanbag.
  4. Whoever grabs it tries to run it home across their sideline. The other player tries to tag them.
  5. 1 point for a successful return OR 1 point for a tag. Reset and call a new number. Call two numbers for 2-v-2.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Steal the Bacon for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Reacts on the number.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out or defends a line — one tactic, not a crowd around the object.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Tags the carrier, does not grab clothes.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the object.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

Source

65. Stones

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 5 min · poly spots or beanbags as stones.

Two teams cross to steal stones, and a teammate walks a tagged player home. Use it to practise attacking, defending and helping a teammate.

Equipment: Beanbags (stones), hoops, pinnies.

Set-up: Two ends. Stones in each home hoop. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Cross to steal a stone and return it.
  2. Tagged players sit. A teammate links arms and walks them home — both are safe on that walk.
  3. Count stones at the whistle.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Play Stones in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • One stone at a time.
  • A tag sends the stone back.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must hop the finished path.
  • Colour stones to colour nests.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Stones.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Scatter stones. Students collect one at a time to a nest, or move stones to build a path across the ‘river.’
  • Taggers return a carried stone to the scatter.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Stones for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. No sliding a piece into a person.
  • Skill focus. One object per trip.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the stone.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Add a jail hoop that needs two rescuers.

Safety. No body checks. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

66. Switch

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class · 4 min · spots or hoops.

On “Switch,” corner players change spots while the middle player tries to claim an empty corner. Use it to practise quick feet with no contact.

Equipment: 4 cones, hoops, or mats as corners. Optional centre pylon.

Set-up: Groups of 5: one player in the middle, four on corners. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. On “Switch,” corner players must change spots.
  2. The middle player tries to claim an empty corner.
  3. The player left without a corner becomes the new middle.
  4. First foot on the spot keeps it. No pushing.
  5. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  6. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • On ‘switch,’ find a new home.
  • The leftover caller is not out.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Call a colour to switch.
  • Must skip to the new spot.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Switch.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • One fewer spot than students, or one extra caller.
  • On the call, everyone must change spots. The leftover gives the next call.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Switch for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Finds a group or a spot without diving.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out and moves to an open corner — not a crowd at one spot.
  • Skill focus. Leftovers stay in — they join the teacher or the next round.
  • Character Development. Honest about who reached the spot first; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a classmate’s name; includes a player who has not had a turn in the middle.
Grades 1–2. Walk the switch. Teacher calls.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Students call Switch. Mats only in gymnastics month — quiet feet.

Safety. No body checks. If two arrive, rock-paper-scissors, both stay in. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

67. Team Tag

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Two colours · 5 min.

The tag team passes a ball to get close and tags with the ball in hand, and tagged players join the tag team. Use it as an invasion lead-up that rewards passing.

Equipment: 1 playground ball or pinnie, bag of extra pinnies.

Set-up: 3–4 start as the tag team in pinnies. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. The tag team may only tag with a ball they are holding — no throws, no walking with the ball.
  2. They pass to move the ball closer, then tag.
  3. Tagged players put on a pinnie and join the tag team.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Tag the other colour only.
  • A tag switches your colour or sends you to a stretch.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Must be holding a ball to tag.
  • Safe hoops for 5 seconds.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Team Tag.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Pinnies on one team. Soft tag the other colour.
  • House rule: a tag is a pinnie swap or a 8-jump stretch, then stay in.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Team Tag for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. A tag is a short skill, then back in.
  • Skill focus. Spreads out, passes then moves, or defends a line — one tactic, not a crowd around the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score and honest tags; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Soft tag.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Allow 2 steps with the ball.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. Tag with the ball in two hands, not a shove. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

68. The Perfect Pass

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 16–25 · Two teams · 4-minute games · set a pass number (3–5).

Teams must complete 3 (or 5) passes before they can score, and an interception resets the count. Use it to practise passing and getting open.

Equipment: Soccer or playground ball, pinnies, two end lines or hoops.

Set-up: Two teams. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Carrier is frozen.
  2. Score only after 3 (or 5) completes.
  3. Intercepts reset the count.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Count the passes out loud.
  • Carrier is frozen.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Everyone on the team must touch.
  • Non-dominant-hand passes only for one round.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in The Perfect Pass.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Score only after the set number of completed passes.
  • The player with the ball cannot run.

Video. The Perfect Pass (Prime Coaching Sport)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in The Perfect Pass for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Carrier pivots — no running with the ball.
  • Skill focus. Looks for a teammate before a score.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Five completes or a 3-second hold.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

69. Through the Gates

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 5–16 · Pairs · 6 min · cone gates all over the gym.

Partners send a ball through a cone gate, then trap it and send it back. Use it to practise accurate kicking or push-passing.

Equipment: Cone pairs as gates, soccer balls.

Set-up: 6–10 gates. Partners. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Name the two jobs (attack / defend or the two end-lines) and which ball we are using.
  2. Send through a gate. Partner traps and sends back.
  3. Count fair gates, not speed.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Plant, inside foot, trap on the other side.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must use the weak foot.
  • A defender shadows one gate.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Through the Gates.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • 8–12 cone gates. Pairs: one kicks through, one traps, then swap.
  • Count gates in 60 seconds, then change partners.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Through the Gates for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Ball stays close; trap or dribble under control.
  • Skill focus. One ball at a time; looks before sending.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

70. Traffic Lights (kick)

Invasion & team

When: October, January · Slot: 5–16 or 0–5 · Whole class · 4–5 min · one sport ball each if you have them.

Students dribble on green, trap on yellow and stop with a foot on the ball on red. Use it to practise ball control and listening.

Equipment: Soccer balls, one per student if you can.

Set-up: Teach green / yellow / red with the sole trap. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Name the two jobs (attack / defend or the two end-lines) and which ball we are using.
  2. Green: dribble. Yellow: trap and wait. Red: stop, foot on the ball.
  3. Restart on green.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Green dribble, yellow trap, red sole-stop.
  • Eyes up.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • Add blue = change direction.
  • No balls: locomotor only.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Traffic Lights (kick).
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: October, January — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Scatter. Each student with a sport ball, or share 1:2.
  • Call colours. Add weak-foot on a second round.

71. Wall Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · 2–3 out, rest as wall goalies · 6 min.

Small teams play while everyone else stands along their end wall as the goal. Use it to practise passing and shooting with the whole class involved.

Equipment: Soccer ball, pinnies, cones.

Set-up: 2–3 field players per team. Everyone else stands along their end wall as a goal. Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Field players pass and shoot at the opposite wall of teammates.
  2. Wall players may only stop the ball with their feet or body — they are the goal.
  3. Rotate field players every 2–3 minutes.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.
  5. Play Wall Soccer in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Pass then shoot.
  • Wall goalies stay on the line.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.

Variations

  • One-touch finish.
  • Must shoot below hip height.
  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Wall Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Two or three attackers try to score on a wall of classmates who may only save, not leave the line.
  • Rotate attackers every 60–90 seconds.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Wall Soccer for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Ball stays close; trap or dribble under control.
  • Skill focus. One ball at a time; looks before sending.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. Heads up. No slide tackles or body checks. Equipment stays below the agreed height.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. No keepers named; big wall. Walk-dribble allowed.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

72. Wild Ball Soccer

Invasion & team

When: September · Slot: 16–25 · Large group · K-8 · 5–8 min rounds.

Play soccer with several soft objects at once, such as a foam football, a large foam die and a beach ball. Use it at any grade to practise footwork and keeping your head up.

Also called: Crazyball Soccer

Equipment: Various soft or foam balls

Set-up: Set up soccer as usual, then add extra “crazy” kickable objects at the same time (foam football, large foam die, beach ball, foam soccer balls). Pinnies on one team. Show the two attacking directions before GO. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Feet only on all objects. Choose whether goalies and regular goals still count.
  2. Every object that crosses a goal line can score, or assign different points to different objects.
  3. Keep heads up — many bouncing objects. Foam / soft equipment only.
  4. Play 4–6 minute periods. Extra players are bumpers, walls, or the next wave — they stay in a job.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Head up. Pass or move — do not crowd the ball.
  • Find a passing lane, not a pile-up.
  • Soft send; the ball stays below the chest unless the sport needs a high set.
  • Small touches. Foot stays near the ball.

Variations

  • Must complete one pass before a shot or a score.
  • Add a second foam ball if more than 24 students so more players get a touch.
  • Extra players are walking walls — they may deflect a ball with feet but may not score.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Wild Ball Soccer.
  • Watch for one student holding the ball. Cue a pass or a 3-second pivot.
  • Year plan: September — match the month’s sport ball when you can.

Video. Wild Ball Soccer (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades K-8

Equipment: Various soft or foam balls

A soccer game for any grade. Set up soccer as usual, but add other soft kickable objects along with the soccer ball, such as foam footballs, large foam dice or beach balls, all at the same time. Add your own rules for goalies and points.

  1. Set up soccer as usual, then add extra soft kickable objects at the same time (foam football, large foam die, beach ball, foam soccer balls).
  2. Feet only on all objects. Choose whether goalies and regular goals still count.
  3. Every object that crosses a goal line can score, or assign different points to different objects.
  4. Keep heads up — many bouncing objects. Foam / soft equipment only.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Wild Ball Soccer for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the inside of the foot or a taught kick; looks up before sending.
  • Skill focus. Moves to space after a pass; does not crowd the ball.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role (attacker, defender, wall, sideline) and rotates without fuss.
  • Character Development. Honest score; tries again after a miss.
  • Safety. No slide tackles; foam or school soccer ball as listed.
  • Healthy Relationships. Calls a teammate’s name; includes a player who has not touched the ball.
Grades 1–2. Small space, big targets, walking or jogging. Teacher may be a walking bumper. Hands may help a trap if the ball is soccer.
Grades 3–4. Full game as written, foam or real sport ball as the unit says. One pass before a score if the class crowds.
Grades 5–6. Student captains or officials. Offside-lite or a must-pass rule. Keep score without ranking classmates.

Safety. No slide tackles, no body checks. The ball is not thrown at a face. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

Source

75. Battleships

Target & send

When: January, March · Slot: 16–25 · 2–4 teams · 8–10 min · pins, playground balls, optional standing mats.

Four teams throw playground balls to knock down other teams’ pins while protecting their own. Use it to practise an accurate send and quick defending.

Equipment: 4 hoops, 4 pins, playground balls, cones for zones.

Set-up: Four squares. A pin in a hoop at the back of each. Teams stay in their zone. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Throw playground balls at other pins. 5-second hold.
  2. Stand a pin back up as soon as it falls. Count knockdowns at the whistle.
  3. Nobody stands in their own hoop.
  4. Balls come back along the side. Restart on a teacher call, not a scramble through the middle.
  5. Play Battleships in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Stay in your zone.
  • Reset a pin; do not throw it back.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Opposite-foot step. Eyes on the target, not the teacher.
  • Wait for the fetch before the next throw.
  • One object in the air at a time per pair or lane.

Variations

  • Four-corner ships.
  • Must hit the pin on a bounce.
  • Step back one giant step after each make (grades 3–6).
  • Opposite hand or weaker foot for the last round.
  • Team score is ‘makes in two minutes’ — not a ranking of throwers.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Battleships.
  • Watch for fetchers walking through the throwing line. Make them a job to the side.
  • Year plan: January, March — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Each team has a pin in a hoop (the ship) and a throwing line.
  • Throw playground balls to knock other ships. A downed pin is stood up by that team after a count of 3, or you keep a knockdown tally.

Video. Battleship over a mat wall (Benjamin Pirillo)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Battleships for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Underhand or below-shoulder send to a target.
  • Skill focus. Chooses a target and a force — close and soft, or far and firm — not a wild throw.
  • Skill focus. Waits a turn; collects on the call so the next player can send.
  • Character Development. Records or resets honestly; a miss is another try.
  • Safety. Fetchers are not in the throwing lane.
  • Healthy Relationships. Cheers a classmate’s send; does not block someone from a turn.
Grades 1–2. Short throwing line. Underhand or roll is allowed. Teacher fetches with the first group.
Grades 3–4. Standard distance. Opposite-foot step. Fetchers stay to the side.
Grades 5–6. Catch a thrown ball to freeze that thrower for a 10-count.

Safety. Throwing line is closed until fetchers are to the side. One object in the air per lane. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

77. Bottle Flip

Target & send

When: December · Slot: 8-15 · Daly Exercise · slot 8-15.

Flip the bottle so it stands in box 1, then 2, then 3. No skipping. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Daly Exercise: Bottle Flip

Equipment: Part-filled bottles; floor markers or saucer cones for six boxes

Set-up: Mark six boxes in a line. A little water in each bottle.

How we play

  1. Flip in box 1.
  2. Upright = move to the next box.
  3. On its side = stay. Partner turn.
  4. First upright landing in box 6 wins.
  5. Freeze on the whistle. Reset kit, then the next round.

If this happens

  • Kit knocks a person: pause, widen space, restart.
  • Argument over a point: teacher call, play on.
  • Someone sits out after a miss: they rejoin on the next turn with a short task instead.

How a round ends. Play 3–6 minutes, or first team/player to the posted target, then rotate jobs.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • One job at a time. Extra players fetch or count.
  • A miss is a short task, then back in.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Two or three boxes. Two-hand set-down counts.
  • Six boxes. One-hand flip.
  • Time cap or team relay.

Teaching tips

  • Source: Daly Exercise. Same house rules as the rest of the playbook.
  • Watch for crowding at one piece of kit. Add a second station before you add speed.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Bottle Flip for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the taught action in Bottle Flip with control, not a rush that knocks kit or people.
  • Skill focus. Chooses space, timing, or a partner job in Bottle Flip instead of crowding.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares kit in Bottle Flip.
  • Character Development. Honest about a miss or a line in Bottle Flip.
  • Safety. Freeze on the whistle. No throws at a face.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a classmate try again after a miss.
Grades 1–2. Two or three boxes. Two-hand set-down counts.
Grades 3–4. Six boxes. One-hand flip.
Grades 5–6. Time cap or team relay.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. A miss is a short task, then stay in.

78. Catch Clap

Target & send

When: September, March · Slot: 8-15

Clap once, then catch. Fakes and drops are misses.

Equipment: One ball

Set-up: Teacher in the middle, class evenly spaced.

How we play

  1. Throw to a pupil.
  2. They must clap then catch.
  3. Fake throw plus clap, drop, or catch with no clap = miss, then rejoin.
Grades 1–2. Two claps.
Grades 3–4. Pupil becomes thrower.
Grades 5–6. Score personal catches.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle.

81. Chain Train

Target & send

When: December, February · Slot: 16-25 · Daly Exercise · slot 16-25.

Hit your first hoop, then steal another group's hoop. Every group keeps one. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Daly Exercise: Chain Train

Equipment: 4 hoops and 1 bean bag per group of 4

Set-up: Four hoops in a short line. Last hoop cannot be raided.

How we play

  1. Throw the bag into your first hoop.
  2. Hit = take one hoop from another group.
  3. A group down to one hoop is safe.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset kit, then the next round.

If this happens

  • Kit knocks a person: pause, widen space, restart.
  • Argument over a point: teacher call, play on.
  • Someone sits out after a miss: they rejoin on the next turn with a short task instead.

How a round ends. Play 3–6 minutes, or first team/player to the posted target, then rotate jobs.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • One job at a time. Extra players fetch or count.
  • A miss is a short task, then back in.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • No stealing. Add from a central pile.
  • Steal allowed. Must keep one hoop.
  • Must hit the newest hoop before you steal.

Teaching tips

  • Source: Daly Exercise. Same house rules as the rest of the playbook.
  • Watch for crowding at one piece of kit. Add a second station before you add speed.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Chain Train for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the taught action in Chain Train with control, not a rush that knocks kit or people.
  • Skill focus. Chooses space, timing, or a partner job in Chain Train instead of crowding.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares kit in Chain Train.
  • Character Development. Honest about a miss or a line in Chain Train.
  • Safety. Freeze on the whistle. Soft tags. No throws at a face.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a classmate try again after a miss.
Grades 1–2. No stealing. Add from a central pile.
Grades 3–4. Steal allowed. Must keep one hoop.
Grades 5–6. Must hit the newest hoop before you steal.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. Soft two-finger or noodle tags on the back or shoulder — never the head. A miss is a short task, then stay in.

85. FLY BACK

Target & send

When: January, March · Slot: 16–25 · Whole class or two teams · 6 min · sport ball or beanbag.

A runner takes a disc from the safe zone and throws it to teammates, who keep it only if they catch it. Use it to practise throwing and catching under a little pressure.

Equipment: Discs or balls, cones, pinnies.

Set-up: Two teams. A far safe zone holds the discs. A home catching area for each team. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. A runner crosses, picks one disc from the safe zone, and throws it toward teammates.
  2. A catch at home keeps the disc. A drop sends it back.
  3. First team to catch its set, or play to a whistle.
  4. Balls come back along the side. Restart on a teacher call, not a scramble through the middle.
  5. Play FLY BACK in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Throw, fly out, come back on a catch.
  • Call it.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Opposite-foot step. Eyes on the target, not the teacher.
  • Wait for the fetch before the next throw.
  • One object in the air at a time per pair or lane.

Variations

  • Must catch one-handed for a bonus (Grade 5–6).
  • Step back one giant step after each make (grades 3–6).
  • Opposite hand or weaker foot for the last round.
  • Team score is ‘makes in two minutes’ — not a ranking of throwers.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in FLY BACK.
  • Watch for fetchers walking through the throwing line. Make them a job to the side.
  • Year plan: January, March — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • A thrower sends the ball. Fielders catch to send the thrower ‘back’ or to score a team point.
  • Use it as a Newcomb cousin without a net: catch and throw to a home hoop.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in FLY BACK for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Catch, then throw — call it.
  • Skill focus. Chooses a target and a force — close and soft, or far and firm — not a wild throw.
  • Skill focus. Waits a turn; collects on the call so the next player can send.
  • Character Development. Records or resets honestly; a miss is another try.
  • Safety. Sends toward the target, not a face. Walks to collect. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Uses a name or ‘mine’ before the send.
Grades 1–2. Walk the cross. Beanbags. No defender.
Grades 3–4. Standard distance. Opposite-foot step. Fetchers stay to the side.
Grades 5–6. Add a defender who may intercept in the air only.

Safety. Throwing line is closed until fetchers are to the side. One object in the air per lane. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

86. Flying Saucers

Target & send

When: January, March · Slot: 16–25 · Pairs or teams · 6 min · discs or rings.

Students send a disc or a long underhand throw at hoops placed at two or three distances. Use it to practise accuracy and safe fetching.

Equipment: Discs or rings, hoops, cones.

Set-up: Targets at two or three distances. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. Throwing line behind a cone. Fetchers wait to the side — never in the throwing lane.
  2. Send a disc at a hoop. Fetch behind the line.
  3. Team count of fair lands.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset spots, then the next round.
  5. Play Flying Saucers in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Release flat.
  • Fetchers wait behind a line.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Opposite-foot step. Eyes on the target, not the teacher.
  • Wait for the fetch before the next throw.
  • One object in the air at a time per pair or lane.

Variations

  • Catch in a scoring hoop (Frisbee Flingers).
  • Opposite-hand sends.
  • Step back one giant step after each make (grades 3–6).
  • Opposite hand or weaker foot for the last round.
  • Team score is ‘makes in two minutes’ — not a ranking of throwers.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Flying Saucers.
  • Watch for fetchers walking through the throwing line. Make them a job to the side.
  • Year plan: January, March — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • Send discs at hoop targets. Fetch only after the whistle or from a safe line.
  • Count hoop hits as a team.

  • Movement Skill Development. Catch, then throw — call it.
  • Skill focus. Chooses a target and a force — close and soft, or far and firm — not a wild throw.
  • Skill focus. Waits a turn; collects on the call so the next player can send.
  • Character Development. Records or resets honestly; a miss is another try.
  • Safety. Sends toward the target, not a face. Walks to collect. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Uses a name or ‘mine’ before the send.
Grades 1–2. Short throwing line. Underhand or roll is allowed. Teacher fetches with the first group.

87. Frisbee Flingers

Target & send

When: January, March · Slot: 16–25 · Pairs or two teams · 6–8 min.

Teams complete disc throws and catches, and a catch in a hoop scores. Use it to practise disc throwing and catching.

  • Watch for fetchers walking through the throwing line. Make them a job to the side.

  • Complete a catch in a scoring hoop or end zone.
  • The thrower cannot run. The receiver may move.

  • Active Living. Stays in Frisbee Flingers for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Catch, then throw — call it.
  • Skill focus. Chooses a target and a force — close and soft, or far and firm — not a wild throw.
  • Skill focus. Waits a turn; collects on the call so the next player can send.
  • Character Development. Records or resets honestly; a miss is another try.
  • Safety. Sends toward the target, not a face. Walks to collect. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Uses a name or ‘mine’ before the send.
Grades 1–2. Short throwing line. Underhand or roll is allowed. Teacher fetches with the first group.
Grades 3–4. Standard distance. Opposite-foot step. Fetchers stay to the side.

88. Hand Tennis

Target & send

When: November, January, March · Slot: 8-15

Win the rally with hand hits, one bounce per side.

Equipment: Soft ball; a line or bench as net

Set-up: Mark a small court. One server.

How we play

  1. Underhand serve to land on the other side.
  2. Return with the hand.
  3. More than one bounce or out = point.
Grades 1–2. Allow two bounces.
Grades 3–4. Bat or racket instead of a hand.
Grades 5–6. Doubles.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle.

89. Hockey pin knockdown

Target & send

When: January · Slot: —

One controlled shot after a dribble.

Equipment: Tunnel (two lines of cones, or a hoop in a hula hoop stand), pin, stick, hockey ball.

Set-up: Tunnel to a pin.

How we play

  1. Dribble the tunnel. One push at the pin. Dribble home.
Grades 1–2. Kick the hockey ball if no sticks.
Grades 3–4. One shot.
Grades 5–6. Receive a partner pass before the shot.

Safety. No high sticks. Fetch from the side.

90. Hoop Target Bowling

Target & send

When: October, January, June · Slot: 8-15

Land an overarm throw in a hoop, seated or standing.

Equipment: Floor hoops, soft balls

Set-up: Hoops at mixed distances. Throwers in front.

How we play

  1. Overarm into the hoop.
  2. Coach the up-back and down-through arm path.
  3. Freeze on the whistle.
Grades 1–2. Shorter distance, larger hoop.
Grades 3–4. Two-hand pass first.
Grades 5–6. Wall target if floor hoops are hard to see.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle.

91. Human Catapult

Target & send

When: January, March · Slot: 8-15 · Daly Exercise · slot 8-15.

The sit-up is the throw. Accuracy after a sit-up is the skill. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Daly Exercise: Human Catapult

Equipment: Mat, soft ball, cone behind each partner

Set-up: Partners sit back-to-back. Cone behind each, out of the face line.

How we play

  1. A sit-up throws at B's cone.
  2. Knockdown = 1. Reset.
  3. Swap. Freeze on the whistle.

If this happens

  • Kit knocks a person: pause, widen space, restart.
  • Argument over a point: teacher call, play on.
  • Someone sits out after a miss: they rejoin on the next turn with a short task instead.

How a round ends. Play 3–6 minutes, or first team/player to the posted target, then rotate jobs.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • One job at a time. Extra players fetch or count.
  • A miss is a short task, then back in.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Seated chest-pass. No sit-up required.
  • Sit-up then throw.
  • Non-dominant throw.

Teaching tips

  • Source: Daly Exercise. Same house rules as the rest of the playbook.
  • Watch for crowding at one piece of kit. Add a second station before you add speed.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Human Catapult for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the taught action in Human Catapult with control, not a rush that knocks kit or people.
  • Skill focus. Chooses space, timing, or a partner job in Human Catapult instead of crowding.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares kit in Human Catapult.
  • Character Development. Honest about a miss or a line in Human Catapult.
  • Safety. Freeze on the whistle. No throws at a face.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a classmate try again after a miss.
Grades 1–2. Seated chest-pass. No sit-up required.
Grades 3–4. Sit-up then throw.
Grades 5–6. Non-dominant throw.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. A miss is a short task, then stay in.

92. Knock the Pin

Target & send

When: November, May, June · Slot: 8-15

Run the triangle, then throw to knock the centre pin.

Equipment: Cones in a triangle; centre cone in a hoop; softballs

Set-up: Start cones form a triangle around a hoop-and-cone target.

How we play

  1. Run around the outside of your cone, return, throw from the cone.
  2. Hit = point and reset.
  3. Freeze on the whistle.
Grades 1–2. Shorter triangle.
Grades 3–4. Underarm only.
Grades 5–6. Team total.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle.

93. Low-ball

Target & send

When: January, March · Slot: 16–25 · 3–5 even teams or pairs · 8–12 minutes · fetchers stay to the side of the throwing line.

Students send dodgeballs at feet or shins only, and a hit player does a 10-second stretch, then returns. Use it to practise a controlled, low throw.

Equipment: 4–6 dodgeballs, centre line.

Set-up: Two halves. Balls start on the line. Agree the freeze signal before the first round.

How we play

  1. On the whistle, send balls at opponents’ feet or shins only.
  2. A catch is a save. A below-waist hit: 10-second stretch on your sideline, then back in.
  3. Balls that go high are dead. Pick them up and start again.
  4. Balls come back along the side. Restart on a teacher call, not a scramble through the middle.
  5. Play Low-ball in short rounds so more students get the key job, then freeze and reset.

Cues

  • Feet and shins.
  • Stretch, then back in.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Opposite-foot step. Eyes on the target, not the teacher.
  • Wait for the fetch before the next throw.
  • One object in the air at a time per pair or lane.

Variations

  • Roll only for 1–2.
  • Step back one giant step after each make (grades 3–6).
  • Opposite hand or weaker foot for the last round.
  • Team score is ‘makes in two minutes’ — not a ranking of throwers.

Teaching tips

  • Reset: freeze, walk back to start spots, change the key role, then GO. Two or three short rounds beat one long messy one in Low-ball.
  • Watch for fetchers walking through the throwing line. Make them a job to the side.
  • Year plan: January, March — match the month’s sport ball when you can.
  • A high ball is dead. A hit is 10 seconds, not an out.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Low-ball for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Sends below the waist.
  • Skill focus. Chooses a target and a force — close and soft, or far and firm — not a wild throw.
  • Skill focus. Waits a turn; collects on the call so the next player can send.
  • Character Development. Records or resets honestly; a miss is another try.
  • Safety. Re-enters after the stretch.
  • Healthy Relationships. Cheers a classmate’s send; does not block someone from a turn.
Grades 1–2. Short throwing line. Underhand or roll is allowed. Teacher fetches with the first group.
Grades 3–4. Standard distance. Opposite-foot step. Fetchers stay to the side.
Grades 5–6. Step back after a make. Weaker hand or weaker foot on the last round. A student official on the line.

Safety. Throwing line is closed until fetchers are to the side. One object in the air per lane. Foam dodgeballs. Hits below the waist. A high ball is dead. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one.

Target & send

Dodgeball with a net and a goalie at each end.
