232. RPS Warm-up

Fitness & landings

When: February, April · Slot: 16–25 · Pairs · home lines · no kit

Rock-paper-scissors decides who chases or who takes the object.

Also called: RPS Rounders · Carraig Páipéar Siosúr

Equipment: None · optional cone between pairs

Set-up: Pairs meet at the centre. Home lines behind each.

How we play

  1. Play one RPS on a count of three.
  2. Winner chases loser toward the loser’s home line.
  3. Tag before the line = point to the winner. Safe over the line = point to the runner.
  4. New partner next bout.

If this happens

  • RPS argument: teacher calls it, play on.
  • Push instead of a tag: no point.
  • Need a classroom version: Boom / Clap / Snatch on an object between two seated pupils.

How a round ends. Best of five bouts, or 3–5 minutes.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Quiet landings. Bend ankles, knees, and hips.
  • Quality beats speed. Stop if a wrist, knee, or back complains.
  • Breathe out on the work; do not hold a breath to rush.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Hop hoop-to-hoop until you meet, then RPS (Daly hop version).
  • Cone between pairs; grab on the word ‘cone.’
  • RPS Rounders: winner bats, loser fields a short diamond.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: RPS Rounders; Carraig Páipéar Siosúr.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. RPS Warm-up (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 1-8

Equipment: 4 cones

A simple warm-up that becomes an instant activity once students know it. It needs only 4 cones, works almost anywhere, and suits large groups.

  1. Mark a centre meeting strip and two home end lines with 4 cones.
  2. Students pair up and meet at the centre. They play one round of Rock-Paper-Scissors.
  3. Winner chases loser toward the loser’s home line. A tag before the line scores for the winner; reaching the line first scores for the runner.
  4. Both jog back to centre and play again immediately.
  5. After 2–3 minutes, switch partners and repeat.

Source · Video

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in the bout for the set time; names breath or warmth after.
  • Movement Skill Development. Matches the called movement (run, skip, jump, freeze).
  • Skill focus. Paces the set so they can finish, not sprint and collapse.
  • Skill focus. Works beside a partner or in a hoop without bumping others out.
  • Character Development. Records a private number honestly, or skips the number and names effort.
  • Safety. Soft landings; freeze on the signal.
  • Healthy Relationships. Lets a classmate have a hoop or spot; no blocking a landing.
Grades 1–2. Half the distance or fewer reps. Knees-down options. Stop if wrists or backs complain.
Grades 3–4. Full lane or full set. Quiet landings. Partner counts.
Grades 5–6. Show three quality reps. Add a half-turn, opposite hand, or a hoop target on the last set.

Safety. Quiet landings. Stop a station if a wrist, knee, or back complains — switch to a walk option. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

233. Safe to Shore

Fitness & landings

When: February, April · Slot: 16–25 · Start and finish lines · hoops or mats as islands · one spare per team if you want the hard version

The floor is out. Cross using only the islands.

Also called: Cross the River · Ice Floe Crossing · Flip Flop

Equipment: Hoops or mats as islands · start and finish lines

Set-up: Islands between the lines. Show a legal step. Agree the restart: full restart or last safe island.

How we play

  1. Slide or lift islands forward.
  2. A foot on the floor outside an island = back to the last safe island.
  3. All people and all islands must finish.

If this happens

  • Spare hoop left behind: they need it — send someone back.
  • Island pile-up: one quiet leader talks.

How a round ends. First team fully across, or all groups succeed.

Cues

  • New path if you touch the ‘water.’
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Quiet landings. Bend ankles, knees, and hips.
  • Quality beats speed. Stop if a wrist, knee, or back complains.
  • Breathe out on the work; do not hold a breath to rush.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Classmates are tunnels and bridges; the other half travels, then swap.
  • Carry a built 6-hoop hut without collapse (Hoop Hut — keep as its own card if the build is the lesson).
  • Ice Floe Crossing / Flip Flop: flip mats as you go.
  • Cross the River: same islands, one spare hoop.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Bridges & Rivers; Cross the River; Ice Floe Crossing; Flip Flop.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. River Crossing (PE Game Changers)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Safe to Shore for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Crosses on the call, not after a wait.
  • Skill focus. Paces the set so they can finish, not sprint and collapse.
  • Skill focus. Works beside a partner or in a hoop without bumping others out.
  • Character Development. Records a private number honestly, or skips the number and names effort.
  • Safety. Quiet landings; walks on and off mats.
  • Healthy Relationships. Lets a classmate have a hoop or spot; no blocking a turn.
Grades 1–2. Wide islands. Teacher spots.
Grades 3–4. Full lane or full set. Quiet landings. Partner counts.
Grades 5–6. Show three quality reps. Add a half-turn, opposite hand, or a hoop target on the last set.

Safety. Quiet landings. Stop a station if a wrist, knee, or back complains — switch to a walk option. Walk on and off mats. No diving onto a house mat. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

234. Shoot for the Stars

Fitness & landings

When: January, May · Slot: 8-15 · Daly Exercise · slot 8-15.

One rolls at a cone. Partner works behind it. Then swap. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Daly Exercise: Shoot for the Stars

Equipment: One ball and one cone per pair

Set-up: Cone in the middle. Roller faces it. Partner behind it marches or jacks.

How we play

  1. Roll along the floor at the cone.
  2. Hit = 1 and swap.
  3. Miss = retrieve and try again.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset kit, then the next round.

If this happens

  • Kit knocks a person: pause, widen space, restart.
  • Argument over a point: teacher call, play on.
  • Someone sits out after a miss: they rejoin on the next turn with a short task instead.

How a round ends. Play 3–6 minutes, or first team/player to the posted target, then rotate jobs.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • One job at a time. Extra players fetch or count.
  • A miss is a short task, then back in.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • March behind the cone.
  • Jumping jacks. Swap after every hit.
  • Two-cone gate.

Teaching tips

  • Source: Daly Exercise. Same house rules as the rest of the playbook.
  • Watch for crowding at one piece of kit. Add a second station before you add speed.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Shoot for the Stars for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Uses the taught action in Shoot for the Stars with control, not a rush that knocks kit or people.
  • Skill focus. Chooses space, timing, or a partner job in Shoot for the Stars instead of crowding.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares kit in Shoot for the Stars.
  • Character Development. Honest about a miss in Shoot for the Stars.
  • Safety. Freeze on the whistle. No throws at a face.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a classmate try again after a miss.
Grades 1–2. March behind the cone.
Grades 3–4. Jumping jacks. Swap after every hit.
Grades 5–6. Two-cone gate.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle. A miss is a short task, then stay in.

235. Spot-Jump Path

Fitness & landings

When: April, May, June · Slot: 16–25 · Spots, hoops, or a river line · clear landing

Jump the targets. Quiet stick. Quality before speed.

Also called: Spot-jump path · Helicopter / Snake rope · Helicopter · Frogs & Toads · Hurdle hops

Equipment: Spots, hoops, or a two-rope river · optional noodle

Set-up: Lane of spots or a two-rope river. Show two-foot take-off and a still landing.

How we play

  1. Jump target to target. Land inside, not on the rim.
  2. Miss or a noisy fall-back: go back one target.
  3. Walk back along the side. Next jumper.

If this happens

  • Toe over the take-off line: no jump.
  • Lane clash: pause and separate.

How a round ends. First team through, farthest jump, or most quiet sticks.

Cues

  • Two feet.
  • Quiet stick.
  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Quiet landings. Bend ankles, knees, and hips.
  • Quality beats speed. Stop if a wrist, knee, or back complains.
  • Breathe out on the work; do not hold a breath to rush.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Noodle swept on the floor around a circle (Helicopter).
  • Beat jumping.
  • Standing long jump, cone behind the heels.
  • What’s-the-time jumps toward a caller, then flee.
  • Jump The River: one two-rope river that grows.
  • Hurdle hops: low hurdles, same quiet stick.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Spot-jump path; Jump The River; Helicopter / Snake rope; Helicopter; Frogs & Toads; Hurdle hops.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Alberta PEW outcomes

Pick one or two look-fors per class.

  • Active Living. Stays in Spot-jump path for the set time; can name breath or enjoyment after — not a ranking.
  • Movement Skill Development. Two-foot take-off and a still land.
  • Skill focus. Paces the set so they can finish, not sprint and collapse.
  • Skill focus. Works beside a partner or in a hoop without bumping others out.
  • Character Development. Records a private number honestly, or skips the number and names effort.
  • Safety. One jumper on the path.
  • Healthy Relationships. Lets a classmate have a hoop or spot; no blocking a landing.
Grades 1–2. Half the distance or fewer reps. Knees-down options. Stop if wrists or backs complain. Feet-only if sticks are new.
Grades 3–4. Full lane or full set. Quiet landings. Partner counts.
Grades 5–6. Add a half-turn on the last spot.

Safety. Quiet landings. Stop a station if a wrist, knee, or back complains — switch to a walk option. Sticks stay below the knee. No slapshots. Blade on the floor when you stop. No elimination: a sit is a stretch, a jail, a hospital, or a role swap, then back in. Freeze on the whistle. Watch a demo or clip before the first play if you have one. Tags are two-finger or noodle on the back or shoulder. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher).

236. Stick Tic-Tac-Toe

Fitness & landings

When: April · Slot: 16-25 · Daly Exercise · slot 16-25.

A stuck landing claims the hoop. Three in a row wins. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

Also called: Daly Exercise: Stick Tic-Tac-Toe

Equipment: Nine hoops in a 3x3; agreed shapes

Set-up: Agree legal shapes. One player in the grid at a time.

How we play

  1. Perform the shape and stick the landing in a free hoop.
  2. That hoop is yours. Other team plays.
  3. Three in a row or the grid is full.
  4. Freeze on the whistle. Reset kit, then the next round.

If this happens

  • Kit knocks a person: pause, widen space, restart.
  • Argument over a point: teacher call, play on.

  • One job at a time. Extra players fetch or count.
  • A miss is a short task, then back in.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Step-in counts. Teacher names the shape.
  • Landing must stick.
  • Teacher calls the shape just before they jump.

Teaching tips

  • Source: Daly Exercise. Same house rules as the rest of the playbook.
  • Watch for crowding at one piece of kit. Add a second station before you add speed.

  • Skill focus. Chooses space, timing, or a partner job in Stick Tic-Tac-Toe instead of crowding.
  • Skill focus. Takes a role and shares kit in Stick Tic-Tac-Toe.
  • Character Development. Honest about a miss in Stick Tic-Tac-Toe.
  • Safety. Freeze on the whistle.
  • Healthy Relationships. Helps a classmate try again after a miss.
Grades 1–2. Step-in counts. Teacher names the shape.
Grades 3–4. Landing must stick.

237. Tennis Jump

Fitness & landings

Jump so the rolling ball ends up inside the cone between your feet. Daly Exercise game. Nobody sits the period.

238. Throw & Go

Fitness & landings

When: May, June · Slot: 16–25 · Pairs or a circle · 1 ball · space to run a loop

Pass, then both movers relocate so the next pass happens on the move.

Also called: Throw & Go · Beat Ball / Beat the Ball · Beat Ball · Hand It Over · Ball Run

Equipment: 1 ball per pair or circle

Set-up: Show a lead pass. Drop = 3 jumps and continue, not an out.

How we play

  1. A throws a lead pass. B catches. Both run and switch roles.
  2. Continue down the field.
  3. Circle version: pass while music plays; music off and the last thrower races the ball around the outside.

If this happens

  • Who last threw is unclear: the holder when the music stopped runs.
  • Two balls in a line of three is the hard version — teach one ball first.

How a round ends. First pair to the line, or several music stops.

Cues

  • Freeze on the whistle — then eyes on the teacher.
  • Quiet landings. Bend ankles, knees, and hips.
  • Quality beats speed. Stop if a wrist, knee, or back complains.
  • Breathe out on the work; do not hold a breath to rush.

Skins — same game, different wrapper

  • Beat Ball: batter hits, then races the fielded ball around the bases.
  • Hand It Over / Ball Run: same pass-then-move.

Teaching tips

  • Also called: Throw & Go; Beat Ball / Beat the Ball; Beat Ball; Hand It Over; Ball Run.
  • House rules: Freeze on the whistle. A miss is a short task and you stay in. Extra players get a job (bumper, next wave, fetcher). No elimination.

Video. Throw & Go (PhysEdGames)

Clips are demos. Our house rules still apply (no elimination, soft tag; dodgeballs stay on the Dodgeball page).

More details · Grades 3-8

Equipment: Type of ball

A quick warm-up or instant activity. Best outside, but it can work indoors with a smaller group.

  1. Pairs, one ball each, lots of space (best outside).
  2. A throws a lead pass. B catches. Both immediately run and switch roles so the next throw happens on the move.
  3. Continue down the field. On a drop, 3 jumps and keep going — no standing.
  4. Change the throw type each round (bounce, roll, football). Switch partners after 2–3 minutes.

Source · Video

  • Healthy Relationships. Lets a classmate have a spot; no blocking a landing.

Grades 3–4. Full lane or full set. Quiet landings. Partner counts.

239. Turtle Race

Fitness & landings

When: April · Slot: 8-15

Crawl with a cone on the back; first across with it still on wins.

Equipment: 1 cone per racer

Set-up: Start and finish. Turtle position, cone on mid-back.

How we play

  1. On go, crawl.
  2. Cone off = stop, replace, continue.
  3. Freeze on the whistle.
Grades 1–2. Bean bag instead.
Grades 3–4. Head-carry walk.
Grades 5–6. Partner replaces the cone.

Safety. No elimination. Freeze on the whistle.

Pair stations (Grades 3–6)

Use these in the 5–16 minute skill block. Partners, 60–90 seconds, then rotate. Use the real sport ball. Teacher-formed pairs.
